The correct answer is A (No bond of its own is required, because there is no customs broker's bond requirement): There is no customs broker's bond requirement, so holding a license is all the authority the firm needs for its own account. The bonds used on the entries the firm files are the importers' entry bonds, which secure duties, taxes, and fees on those entries. The chapter describes no thirty-day grace period, no client-provided substitute, and no bond waiver issued by CBP, because no broker bond obligation exists in the first place.
  16. The correct answer is D (Class four, bonded bins or parts of buildings for grain): 19 CFR 19.1(a) designates Class 4 as a bonded bin or part of a building specifically used for storing bulk grain. This class accounts for the specialized handling grain requires compared to general merchandise. Bonded grain storage remains subject to the same duty deferral and withdrawal rules as other warehouse classes.
  17. The correct answer is B (Class nine, a duty free store): 19 CFR 19.1(a) designates Class 9 as a duty free store, permitting retail sale of bonded merchandise directly to persons who are about to depart the United States or a qualifying foreign destination. Sales are limited to qualifying departing travelers, and the merchandise never enters domestic commerce. This class is common at international airports and land border crossings.
  18. The correct answer is C (A general purpose zone): Under the Foreign Trade Zones Act and 19 CFR 146, a general purpose zone serves multiple unrelated users at a shared site administered by a zone grantee, while a subzone is a separate area approved for a single company's specific operations that cannot practically be included within an existing general purpose zone. Both zone types offer the same underlying customs benefits. The choice between them depends on the applicant's site and operational needs.
  19. The correct answer is A (A civil monetary penalty of up to 10,000 dollars per transaction under 19 USC 1641.): Transacting customs business without a valid broker's license is handled as a civil matter under 19 USC 1641(b)(6), implemented at 19 CFR 111.4 and assessed under the procedures in 19 CFR Part 111 Subpart E. The penalty can run up to 10,000 dollars for each transaction. The conduct is not a crime and carries no imprisonment. The chapter describes no probationary filing period and no warning letter for this conduct, and the absence of a license does not eliminate the penalty.
  20. The correct answer is D (Each mechanism allows some form of deferred, reduced, or eliminated duty liability under CBP oversight): bonds, bonded warehouses, TIB, and FTZs are all interconnected tools within the broader customs framework that allow importers to defer, reduce, or entirely eliminate duty liability under appropriate CBP bond and regulatory oversight, each with its own specific conditions and limitations. Recognizing this common thread helps candidates understand why these topics are frequently tested together on the CBLE. Mastery of the specific procedural requirements, time limits, and bond mechanics for each mechanism is essential for correctly applying them to realistic import scenarios.
  21. The correct answer is C (Class two, a private bonded warehouse for the owner's goods): 19 CFR 19.1(a) designates Class 2 as a private bonded warehouse used exclusively to store merchandise belonging to the proprietor of the warehouse. This distinguishes it from a public bonded warehouse that may store goods belonging to multiple unrelated importers. The proprietor bears sole responsibility for the bonded merchandise inside.
  22. The correct answer is B (Class one, premises owned or leased by the government): 19 CFR 19.1(a) designates Class 1 as premises owned or leased by the government for storing merchandise pending examination, appraisement, or under seizure. This class is distinct from privately operated bonded facilities used by importers for ordinary commercial storage. CBP retains direct control over Class 1 premises.
  23. The correct answer is D (FTZs generally offer broader authorized processing and inverted tariff benefits): while both bonded warehouses and FTZs can defer duty on stored inventory, FTZs generally provide broader latitude for processing and assembly activity, along with the potential inverted tariff benefit if the assembled product carries a lower duty rate than its components. This makes the FTZ option more attractive specifically when light assembly or manufacturing is part of the plan. Both regimes support duty deferral, so that shared feature alone does not distinguish them.
  24. The correct answer is C (It consists of premises owned or leased by the government itself): a Class 1 bonded warehouse consists of premises owned or leased by the federal government and used under CBP custody for the storage of merchandise, distinguishing it from privately operated classes such as Class 2 or Class 3. This government ownership or lease arrangement reflects a different operational and administrative structure compared to commercially operated bonded facilities. Despite the ownership difference, Class 1 warehouses still operate under the same general bond and recordkeeping framework applicable to bonded storage generally.
  25. The correct answer is A (The license can be placed in an inactive or suspended posture for the administrative lapse.): The chapter states that a broker who fails to timely file the periodic status report can have the license placed in an inactive or suspended posture, separate from any disciplinary action for misconduct. A broker can lose active status purely through an administrative lapse such as a missed status report without ever having engaged in wrongdoing. The chapter does not require revocation, and it does not make a late filing a criminal offense. A client complaint is not a condition for this administrative consequence.
  26. The correct answer is B (No, an STB is generally limited to the one entry it was filed for): a single transaction bond is, by its nature, tied to one specific entry transaction and does not extend automatically to cover subsequent, separate entries, even if filed shortly afterward by the same importer. Any additional entry would require either a new single transaction bond or a continuous bond to obtain coverage. This limited scope is precisely why regular importers generally find continuous bonds more efficient than repeatedly posting single transaction bonds.
  27. The correct answer is D (The value as of the date of the withdrawal for consumption): consistent with the rule that duty rate is fixed at the time of withdrawal, the dutiable value used for calculating duty on warehoused merchandise is likewise generally determined as of the date of withdrawal for consumption, not the original importation date. This ensures duty is assessed based on conditions applicable when the goods finally enter US commerce. Brokers must recalculate both rate and value carefully at the time of each withdrawal rather than relying on figures from the original warehouse entry.
  28. The correct answer is A (Records must be kept readily accessible and producible on reasonable request.): The chapter states that broker records must be kept readily accessible and producible on reasonable request, and it adds that an inability to promptly produce required records during a compliance review is a serious compliance failure. Files held on backup tapes that cannot be retrieved for weeks do not meet that standard. The chapter does not require paper copies, does not require storage at a particular office location, and does not require indexing by the importer's entry number. Electronic storage is permitted when the system meets CBP's standards.
  29. The correct answer is B (Duty deferral for domestic shipments and duty elimination for exports): a distribution center operating within a general-purpose FTZ can realize duty deferral on merchandise eventually entered into US commerce, since duty is not owed until withdrawal, and complete duty elimination on the portion of merchandise that is instead exported to other countries, since that merchandise never enters US commerce. This dual benefit makes FTZs attractive for companies serving both domestic and international customers from one facility. Neither benefit requires a separate bonded warehouse, since the FTZ framework itself provides both.
  30. The correct answer is C (The bond secures a category of future obligations, not one shipment): a continuous bond is structured to secure an ongoing category of obligations, namely all entries the principal files during the bond period, rather than being tied to any single identified shipment at the time of posting. This is precisely why it can efficiently cover future, not-yet-identified importations without requiring a bond amendment for each new entry. Single transaction bonds work differently, being tied specifically to one identified entry from the outset.
  31. The correct answer is C (Warehouse entry is possible but must still satisfy other agency requirements): merchandise subject to other federal agency requirements, such as FDA regulation, may still be entered into a bonded warehouse, but the underlying agency requirements are not waived simply because the goods are under bond. The importer must satisfy both the customs bonded warehouse rules and any applicable agency conditions before the merchandise can ultimately be withdrawn for consumption. This dual compliance obligation is a common point of confusion for importers new to warehouse entry.
  32. The correct answer is A (The supervision duty covers employees who handle client funds as part of customs business.): The chapter lists handling client funds among the activities that fall within a broker's ongoing duty to supervise every person conducting customs business on the broker's behalf. The duty is not limited to preparing entries or classifying merchandise, because it follows the work wherever the brokerage performs it. A separate bank account does not move fund handling outside that duty. CBP does not need to request a formal accounting before the supervision obligation applies.
  33. The correct answer is B (Generally no, zone-restricted status significantly limits later options): zone-restricted status is intended to firmly limit merchandise to export or destruction, and changing out of that status to allow domestic consumption entry is generally not available absent specific CBP approval under defined circumstances. Importers should therefore carefully consider whether zone-restricted status truly fits their intended use before requesting it, since flexibility to later change plans and sell domestically is significantly constrained. This underscores why status election in an FTZ requires careful upfront planning.
  34. The correct answer is D (A warehouse entry defers duty payment until later withdrawal): unlike a consumption entry, which requires duty payment at the time the merchandise is entered for use in domestic commerce, a warehouse entry places merchandise into bonded storage without immediate duty payment, deferring that liability until the merchandise is withdrawn. This is the defining functional difference brokers must understand when advising clients on entry strategy. A bond remains required throughout the warehouse storage period to secure eventual duty payment or proper export.
  35. The correct answer is A (The remedies are not mutually exclusive, so the agency may combine revocation and a penalty.): The chapter states that 19 USC 1641 gives CBP three categories of remedy, namely a monetary penalty, suspension of the license or permit, and revocation. It stresses that these remedies are not mutually exclusive, so CBP can combine a monetary penalty with a suspension or pursue revocation where conduct is severe enough. The objection that the agency must select a single remedy does not match the chapter. The chapter describes no requirement that CBP obtain court approval before combining remedies.
  36. The correct answer is D (Yes, a bond is generally required to secure zone operations): merchandise admitted into an FTZ is generally secured by a customs bond, similar in principle to bonded warehouse merchandise, ensuring compliance with zone procedures and protecting revenue in case merchandise is improperly removed or otherwise mishandled. This bond requirement applies whether the merchandise is in a general-purpose zone or a subzone. It reflects the underlying customs custody concept that continues to apply even though FTZ merchandise is technically considered outside the customs territory for duty purposes.
  37. The correct answer is B (The election is generally irrevocable for that specific merchandise): once privileged foreign status is elected for specific merchandise admitted into an FTZ, the election is generally treated as irrevocable, locking in the classification and rate as of the admission date regardless of subsequent processing. Companies must therefore carefully analyze whether the raw material rate or the eventual finished product rate will be more favorable before making this election. An incorrect election can result in paying a higher duty rate than would otherwise have applied under nonprivileged status.
  38. The correct answer is C (Zone-restricted status, limiting merchandise to export or destruction): merchandise admitted under zone-restricted status may not be entered into US commerce for consumption at all, and may only be exported or destroyed, under 19 CFR 146.44. This status is useful for merchandise held for eventual export, such as goods pending a foreign resale, where the importer wants to avoid any implication that a consumption entry might occur. Zone-restricted status therefore provides the strongest limitation among the FTZ status categories.
  39. The correct answer is D (The paintings must not be sold or used for domestic manufacturing): the fundamental restriction underlying every TIB entry, including artwork brought in for exhibition, is that the merchandise cannot be sold in the United States or used in domestic manufacturing, since the entire duty-free treatment depends on the goods ultimately leaving the country in essentially the same condition. If the gallery wishes to sell a painting, it would need to be entered for consumption with duty paid, converting it out of TIB status. Violating this restriction exposes the TIB bond to liquidated damages.
  40. The correct answer is A (No bond of its own is required, because there is no customs broker's bond requirement): There is no customs broker's bond requirement, and no such bond appears in 19 CFR Part 111, 19 USC 1641, or 19 CFR Part 113. Each client entry is secured by the importer's own entry bond, and carriers, custodians, and foreign trade zone operators hold their own bonds for their own obligations. A brokerage therefore has no bond of its own to obtain, and no deficiency arises from that.
  41. The correct answer is C (Privileged foreign status fixing classification at admission): merchandise admitted to an FTZ under privileged foreign status has its tariff classification and applicable duty rate fixed as of the date of admission into the zone, under 19 CFR 146.41, regardless of any subsequent manufacturing or processing performed within the zone. This election is useful when the raw material's classification carries a lower duty rate than the finished product would otherwise attract. Once elected, privileged status generally cannot be reversed for that merchandise.
  42. The correct answer is B (Proof of exportation submitted to CBP to cancel the bond liability): to properly close a TIB entry, the importer must submit satisfactory proof of exportation, such as a certificate of exportation or other CBP-accepted evidence, allowing CBP to cancel the bond liability tied to that entry. Without this documentation, CBP cannot confirm the merchandise actually left the country, and the bond remains exposed to liquidated damages. Maintaining thorough export records is therefore essential to a successful TIB program.
  43. The correct answer is D (Classification is generally based on the merchandise's condition as manipulated): merchandise withdrawn from a bonded warehouse after authorized manipulation is generally classified and appraised in its condition as manipulated, meaning the repackaged, retail-ready form, rather than its original bulk import condition. This reflects that duty is assessed on merchandise as it actually enters US commerce. Importers should plan manipulation activities with awareness of how they may shift tariff classification or value.
  44. The correct answer is A (CBP reviews character and fitness, and a felony bears on the good moral character standard.): The chapter states that an applicant must demonstrate good moral character, which CBP evaluates through a background investigation, and that passing the examination does not by itself entitle a candidate to a license. The chapter also explains that a conviction of a felony or certain other crimes involving moral turpitude bears on the good character standard required to hold a license. CBP therefore reviews the circumstances rather than granting the license automatically on the examination score. The chapter describes no rule that a past conviction matters only after a client complaint.
  45. The correct answer is B (Professional equipment, tools of trade, and repair components): HTSUS Chapter 98 Subchapter XIII includes provisions for professional equipment and tools of trade brought in temporarily to perform specific work before being taken back out of the country, which fits specialized tooling used for a limited production run. This category recognizes the practical need for businesses to bring specialized equipment across the border temporarily without incurring full duty liability. The dies themselves must ultimately be exported to satisfy the TIB conditions.
  46. The correct answer is D (Relief from duty may be available for merchandise destroyed under bond): when merchandise stored under warehouse entry is destroyed through casualty while still under CBP custody and bond, the importer may seek relief from duty liability since the goods never entered US commerce for consumption. Proper documentation of the destruction, including CBP verification, is required to support this relief. This treatment recognizes that duty is meant to apply to merchandise actually entering domestic commerce.
  47. The correct answer is A (The duty covers every person conducting customs business on the broker's behalf, licensed or not.): The chapter defines responsible supervision and control as the broker's ongoing duty to supervise every person, licensed or unlicensed, who conducts customs business on the broker's behalf. The work covered includes preparing entries, classifying merchandise, calculating duties, communicating with CBP, and handling client funds. A client complaint is not a condition for the duty to apply, and the obligation does not end because an employee holds a license. The chapter treats supervision of the firm's whole operation as the standard rather than oversight limited to one team.
  48. The correct answer is C (One year from the date of the original TIB entry): merchandise entered under Temporary Importation under Bond is generally permitted to remain in the United States for one year from the date of entry, though this period may be extended upon application, subject to an overall statutory maximum. This time-limited nature distinguishes TIB from ordinary consumption entry, where merchandise may remain permanently once duty is paid. Failure to export or destroy the merchandise within the allowed period exposes the bond to liquidated damages.
  49. The correct answer is D (Yes, partial withdrawals for consumption are generally permitted): warehouse entry allows the importer to withdraw merchandise in installments over the storage period, paying duty only on the portion actually withdrawn for consumption at the time of each withdrawal. This flexibility is one of the primary commercial advantages of warehouse entry, letting importers manage cash flow and respond to market demand incrementally. The remaining merchandise stays under bond until it too is withdrawn, exported, or destroyed.
  50. The correct answer is B (Liquidated damages may be assessed against the proprietor's bond): a bonded warehouse proprietor bond secures compliance with warehouse regulations, including proper accounting for and control over stored merchandise. Unauthorized removal without CBP permission and documentation is a bond violation that exposes the proprietor to liquidated damages under 19 CFR 113.63. This reinforces the proprietor's obligation to maintain strict custody and recordkeeping over all merchandise in its bonded facility.
  51. The correct answer is A (CBP conducts a character and fitness review and a background investigation before granting the license.): Passing the examination with a score of seventy-five percent or higher is a threshold requirement, but it does not by itself entitle a candidate to a license. The chapter states that CBP still evaluates good moral character through a background investigation and conducts a character and fitness review before granting the license. The chapter describes no internship requirement and no provisional license issued during a training course. The license is not issued merely because the examination results are posted.
  52. The correct answer is C (No, this exceeds manipulation and would require CBP manufacturing authority): permitted manipulation under 19 CFR 19.11 covers operations like cleaning, sorting, repacking, and similar changes that do not alter the fundamental character of the merchandise. Combining separate lots into a new article changes their identity in a way that goes beyond manipulation and would instead fall under manufacturing operations authorized only in a Class 6 bonded manufacturing warehouse. Warehouse operators must stay within the scope of manipulation authorized for their specific warehouse class.
  53. The correct answer is B (Withdrawal for exportation directly from the bonded warehouse): merchandise stored under warehouse entry may be withdrawn for exportation without payment of duty, since it never entered US commerce for consumption. This avoids the need to pay duty and later pursue a drawback refund, which is a slower and more administratively burdensome path. Exportation withdrawals must be properly documented to confirm the merchandise actually left the country.
  54. The correct answer is B (Class 7, a bonded smelting and refining warehouse): Class 7 warehouses are specifically authorized for smelting and refining imported ores and metals under bond, a specialized industrial process distinct from ordinary storage or general manufacturing. This classification reflects the unique regulatory and safety considerations of metal refining operations. Duty treatment on the resulting refined product depends on whether it is entered for consumption or exported.
  55. The correct answer is D (Bonds may secure liquidated damages tied to violated bond conditions): the conditions listed in the bond, such as timely filing of entry documents or proper redelivery of merchandise, are enforceable through liquidated damages when the principal fails to comply. CBP assesses these damages against the bond amount rather than pursuing a separate contract remedy. This is a core purpose of the bond, protecting revenue and ensuring compliance with entry obligations.
  56. The correct answer is A (The broker must retain power of attorney documentation as part of its required business records): Brokers must retain records relating to their customs business, including powers of attorney, and a broker who cannot produce a valid power of attorney for a client has a serious recordkeeping and authority problem. Storage at the client's office does not satisfy the requirement when the broker cannot retrieve the documents on request. The client's preference does not change the broker's retention duty. CBP does not take permanent custody of broker powers of attorney, and a client statement is not a substitute for the records.
  57. The correct answer is C (It is considered abandoned to the government and subject to sale): under the statutory five-year storage limit codified at 19 CFR 144.36, merchandise remaining in a bonded warehouse beyond five years from the date of importation is considered abandoned and may be disposed of, including by CBP sale, with proceeds applied to duties and charges owed. This time limit prevents indefinite duty deferral and forces owners to make a final disposition decision. No automatic extension exists beyond this fixed period.
  58. The correct answer is C (Class 9, a duty-free store for qualified export sales): a Class 9 warehouse operates as a duty-free store, selling merchandise to individuals who will personally carry it out of the country, which functionally exports the goods and avoids duty liability. These facilities operate under strict CBP controls to confirm the merchandise is delivered to departing travelers rather than entering domestic commerce. This differs sharply from ordinary retail sales subject to normal duty and entry procedures.
  59. The correct answer is B (CBP may require a bond amount above the standard formula for risk): for merchandise subject to antidumping or countervailing duty orders, quota restrictions, or other elevated risk categories, CBP is authorized to require single transaction bond amounts higher than the ordinary entered-value-plus-duties formula. This additional coverage protects revenue given the historical difficulty collecting on such entries. Brokers should always confirm current bond sufficiency guidance before filing in these categories.
  60. The correct answer is A (No, because the duty covers every person conducting customs business on the broker's behalf): The chapter defines responsible supervision and control as the broker's ongoing duty to supervise every person, licensed or unlicensed, who conducts customs business on the broker's behalf. A second license does not remove an employee from the broker's internal control structure. Written procedures, review steps, training, and error correction are the controls CBP expects to see for the operation. Quarterly reviews are not prohibited; the problem is excluding licensed staff from review altogether.
  61. The correct answer is C ($50,000 minimum, or 10 percent of prior year duties if greater): CBP's standard formula sets the continuous bond amount at the greater of $50,000 or 10 percent of the duties, taxes, and fees paid during the preceding twelve months. Here, 10 percent of $300,000 equals $30,000, which is less than the $50,000 floor, so the bond amount defaults to $50,000. Importers with higher duty volumes will see the 10 percent figure exceed the floor and govern instead.
  62. The correct answer is D (The brokerage holds no bond of its own, because the importer's entry bond secures the entries): There is no customs broker's bond requirement, so a continuous importer bond does not displace any bond of the broker's own. The importer's continuous bond secures the importer's own obligations on its entries, while the broker's role is to file accurately and exercise responsible supervision and control. Carriers, custodians, and foreign trade zone operators hold their own bonds for their own obligations, and none of those instruments is held by the brokerage on the importer's behalf.
  63. The correct answer is C (The entity's authority depends on having a licensed officer actively engaged in supervising.): The chapter states that a corporate license belongs to the entity, but the entity's ability to conduct brokerage business lawfully depends on having a licensed, actively engaged officer supervising operations. A corporate license with no actively engaged licensed officer is not functioning lawfully, so the vice president's explanation is wrong. The chapter gives unlicensed officers no authority to assume the licensed officer's duties, and client authorization through a power of attorney cannot supply the missing oversight. The license does depend on licensed personnel in the sense the chapter describes.
  64. The correct answer is D (Further appeal rights follow the judge's decision in the administrative process.): The chapter states that before CBP can suspend or revoke a license or permit, the broker is entitled to notice of the charges and an opportunity to respond, and that for serious matters the referral is to an administrative law judge for a hearing. The chapter adds that further appeal rights follow that decision. The matter therefore does not end at the judge's ruling, and the judge's decision is not final without any review. The chapter describes an administrative process rather than a jury trial in federal district court or a return to the field office for new charges.
  65. The correct answer is B (The corporation needs a licensed officer, not merely a licensed employee, in that role.): The chapter states that every corporate license depends on at least one individually licensed broker who is an officer of the corporation and who is actively engaged in supervising the entity's brokerage operations. A licensed department manager who holds no corporate office does not satisfy that requirement, however experienced she may be. A board resolution or another examination attempt does not change the officer requirement in the chapter. The role must be held by a licensed corporate officer.
  66. The correct answer is A (19 USC 1641(b)(6), which imposes a civil monetary penalty of up to 10,000 dollars per transaction): Once CBP suspends his license, Dominic is transacting customs business without a valid license. That conduct carries a civil monetary penalty of up to 10,000 dollars per transaction under 19 USC 1641(b)(6), implemented at 19 CFR 111.4 and assessed under the penalty procedures in 19 CFR Part 111 Subpart E. The conduct is not a crime and carries no imprisonment. Subpart D governs discipline of licensed brokers, which no longer describes Dominic, and 19 CFR 141.32 addresses powers of attorney rather than penalties.
  67. The correct answer is D (The corporation must maintain responsible supervision and control over all the work): A national permit issued under 19 CFR 111.19 authorizes business throughout the customs territory, and the entity's ability to use that authority depends on responsible supervision and control of the work, including a licensed officer who is actively engaged in supervising the brokerage operations. A single licensed officer can supervise a multi-office operation only if the controls actually provide that oversight. Bonding an office, relying on entry volume, or naming unlicensed managers does not satisfy the standard the chapter describes.
  68. The correct answer is C (Monetary penalties can be substantial per violation, so each entry can carry exposure.): The chapter states that monetary penalties can be substantial per violation, and that repeated or willful violations tend to draw the maximum penalty amounts. Assessing exposure entry by entry fits that description of the remedy under 19 USC 1641. The chapter sets no single cap for a brokerage and does not condition a monetary penalty on also suspending or revoking the license. The penalty is not limited to a calculation based on duties the brokerage failed to remit.
  69. The correct answer is D (It keeps brokers current on regulatory changes as customs law evolves.): The chapter states that individually licensed brokers must complete continuing education as a condition of maintaining an active license, so brokers stay current as customs law evolves. Passing the licensing examination years earlier does not remove that ongoing condition. The chapter does not limit the requirement to supervising brokers, tie it to the licensing examination, or limit it to years when the regulations change. A broker who fails to complete the required education can have the license placed in an inactive or suspended posture.
  70. The correct answer is A (Failing to maintain required records for the retention period established by the rules): The chapter lists failing to maintain required records for the retention period, or to produce them on CBP's request, as a ground for discipline. Correspondence and financial records relating to transactions with clients are among the records brokers must retain, and the general period is five years. A three-year purge destroys records that are still within that period. Supervision, fee splitting, and license transfer are separate grounds that this fact pattern does not describe.
  71. The correct answer is C (Financial records from client transactions are required broker records and must be retained.): The chapter lists financial records relating to transactions with clients and with CBP among the records brokers must retain. A shorter retention period for billing statements and payment ledgers does not match the general five-year rule for broker records. The accounting team's view of what relates to customs work does not remove those financial records from the broker's obligation. Payment in full by the client does not shorten the retention period either.
  72. The correct answer is B (Splitting fees with a person who is not licensed as a customs broker.): The chapter lists splitting fees with a person not licensed as a customs broker in exchange for that person's referral or performance of customs business among the grounds for discipline. The concern is that fee sharing lets unlicensed individuals function as de facto brokers without obtaining a license. Paying Nettle for both referrals and entry paperwork fits that description. The chapter does not treat this arrangement as a due diligence, license transfer, or status reporting issue.
  73. The correct answer is A (The two-year limit applies to partnership grantees, not to a corporate broker grant): A power of attorney granted to a partnership generally must be limited to a period not exceeding two years from execution, though it may be renewed. That two-year rule is specific to partnerships, and individual and corporate brokers are not subject to the same fixed renewal period. A grant to an individual broker is generally unlimited in duration unless it specifies an expiration date or is revoked. The corporate grant does not convert into any other type of authority.
  74. The correct answer is C (It must meet CBP standards for accuracy, retrievability, and integrity.): The chapter states that electronic recordkeeping is permitted if the system meets CBP's standards for accuracy, retrievability, and integrity. A system that loses documents and cannot show whether a file was altered fails those standards, so widespread use of the format does not make it acceptable. The chapter does not require certification by the importer's surety, matching the importer's format, or storing records inside the United States. Electronic retention is allowed, but only on terms that keep the records reliable and producible.
  75. The correct answer is A (The broker's recordkeeping obligation runs independently and cannot be shifted to the importer): Brokers have their own recordkeeping obligations under 19 CFR Part 111, separate from general importer recordkeeping requirements. A broker cannot rely on the importer's separate duty to relieve the broker's own duty. The broker must retain entry records, correspondence, powers of attorney, invoices, bills of lading, and related financial records for the required periods. Shredding the firm's copies after two years falls short of the five-year retention rule and is a violation on its own.
  76. The correct answer is B (It confirms current employment and license status so CBP knows who is practicing.): The chapter states that the status reporting requirement exists so CBP always knows who is actively practicing under a license and where. The report confirms the broker's current employment and license status, which is why it should reflect her new position. The chapter does not describe the filing as reporting entry volume, transferring a license between employers, or listing clients for audits. A broker who fails to file the report timely can have the license placed in an inactive or suspended posture.
  77. The correct answer is C (The practice falls short, because the broker must actually review the work of unlicensed staff.): The chapter says the supervision duty is not satisfied merely by hiring competent staff, and CBP looks at whether the broker actually reviewed the work of unlicensed employees and whether a real quality control process existed. When the same unlicensed writer prepares and approves the entry, no licensed person has checked the filing before or shortly after submission. Documenting the writer's own review does not replace the broker's oversight, and years of experience do not remove the duty. The chapter treats the broker's active involvement as the standard.
  78. The correct answer is D (The duty covers employees who communicate with CBP on the broker's behalf.): The chapter states that responsible supervision and control covers every person conducting customs business on the broker's behalf, including employees who communicate with CBP. The list also includes preparing entries, classifying merchandise, calculating duties, and handling client funds. Because the assistant is speaking for the brokerage, the broker remains responsible for overseeing that work rather than adopting the assistant's recommendations without review. The duty is not limited to formal protests, and it is not satisfied by an annual file review.
  79. The correct answer is C (The corporation must provide licensed oversight of the work done at the second office): The chapter states that an entity's ability to conduct brokerage business lawfully depends on having a licensed officer or qualifying member who is actively engaged in supervising the operations, and that the duty of responsible supervision and control covers every person conducting customs business on the broker's behalf. An unlicensed branch manager filing entries daily is outside that oversight, so the corporation must supply licensed supervision at the second office. Relying on an officer elsewhere, bonding the branch, or paying an additional fee does not satisfy that requirement.
  80. The correct answer is A (It confirms the broker's current employment and license status so CBP knows who is practicing): The status reporting requirement exists so CBP always knows who is actively practicing under a license and where. The report confirms current employment and license status rather than reporting entry volumes or client lists. A broker who fails to timely file the report can have the license placed in an inactive or suspended posture. That administrative outcome is separate from any disciplinary action for misconduct.
  81. The correct answer is C (The firm is entitled to notice of the charges and an opportunity to respond first.): The chapter states that before CBP can suspend or revoke a license or permit, or assess a monetary penalty above a certain threshold, the broker is entitled to notice of the charges and an opportunity to respond. For serious matters the process includes referral to an administrative law judge for a hearing, with further appeal rights afterward. CBP cannot simply impose the penalty without following that statutory procedure. A federal district court hearing before any penalty, and payment followed by a later protest, are not the process the chapter describes.
  82. The correct answer is D (Failing to exercise due diligence about information provided to the government.): The chapter lists, among the grounds for discipline, failing to exercise due diligence in ascertaining the correctness of information provided to a client or to the government. Anika repeated an unverified preference claim to the client without checking any supporting document, which is the due diligence failure the chapter describes. The chapter does not require a financial loss for this ground to apply. The scenario involves no fee splitting, no destroyed records, and no license application statement.
  83. The correct answer is A (The grant remains valid because an individual broker grant lasts until it is revoked or expires): A power of attorney made to a broker who is a natural person is generally treated as unlimited in duration unless it specifies an expiration date or is revoked. The two-year renewal limit applies to partnership grantees, not to individual or corporate brokers. Because the importer has not revoked the grant and the document states no expiration, the authority continues. A new document is not required while the grant remains unrevoked.
  84. The correct answer is B (The officer must be actively engaged in supervising the entity's brokerage operations.): The chapter explains that a corporate license depends on at least one individually licensed broker who is an officer and who is actively engaged in supervising the entity's brokerage operations. A corporate license with no actively engaged licensed officer is not functioning lawfully, no matter how experienced its unlicensed staff may be. Simply keeping a licensed officer on the payroll does not satisfy the active engagement requirement. The role cannot be handed to outside counsel or split between unlicensed managers.

Practice Test 5 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is A (The Houston office requires no further permit action because the national permit covers it): A national permit issued under 19 CFR 111.19 is sufficient permit authority for a broker to conduct customs business throughout the customs territory of the United States. Because the permit is national, opening a staffed office in another city does not require a separate local permit, and the broker does not surrender its existing permit. The firm remains responsible for exercising responsible supervision and control over all work done on its behalf, and client powers of attorney are separate authorizations rather than permit obligations.
  2. The correct answer is C (The signer lacked actual authority to bind the partnership when executing the grant.): The chapter states that a customs power of attorney must be executed by a person with actual authority to bind the principal, which for a partnership means a partner. The office administrator signed without authorization from the partners, so the document does not carry the partnership's authority. Naming the brokerage is proper, since the grant must identify the grantee receiving the authority. The chapter does not require notarization of the signature, and it permits a properly drafted document even when CBP Form 5291 is not used.
  3. The correct answer is A (The license identifies the firm as a broker, while the permit authorizes customs business): A license authorizes a person or entity to be a customs broker, and a permit issued under 19 CFR 111.19 authorizes the broker to conduct customs business. A national permit is required for transacting customs business throughout the customs territory of the United States, and it is sufficient permit authority for that purpose. The two are separate authorities, so a firm that holds only a license has not yet been authorized to transact customs business. The license and permit are not interchangeable, and the permit requirement is not limited to corporate brokerages.
  4. The correct answer is D (Whether the broker reviewed work, was more than nominal, and corrected errors.): The chapter lists the factors CBP examines when it evaluates a broker's supervision, including whether the broker actually reviewed the work of unlicensed employees. CBP also considers whether the broker's involvement was more than nominal, whether a real quality control process existed, and whether the broker took corrective action once problems were identified. Advice offered only when an employee asks does not demonstrate the active oversight the chapter describes. Permit count, firm revenue, and extra professional designations are not among the factors the chapter identifies.
  5. The correct answer is B (Unauthorized customs business and a failure to retain required power of attorney records.): The chapter states that a broker cannot transact customs business for a principal without proper authorization, so filing entries for two years without a signed grant is unauthorized practice for that client. The chapter also states that brokers must retain power of attorney documentation as part of their required business records. A broker who cannot produce a valid power of attorney for a client it has been filing entries for has a serious recordkeeping and authority problem. The chapter does not tie this failure to entry summary timing, importer bond sufficiency, or continuing education.
  6. The correct answer is D (The national permit already covers the new office, so no further permit is needed): A national permit issued under 19 CFR 111.19 is sufficient permit authority for a broker to conduct customs business throughout the customs territory of the United States. Because the firm already holds a national permit, the second office sits inside that authority and does not need a permit of its own. The chapter states no entry volume threshold and no separate permit rule tied to hiring unlicensed entry writers. The broker remains responsible for exercising responsible supervision and control over the work done at both offices.
  7. The correct answer is B (She can be held responsible even without personal knowledge of the specific errors.): The chapter explains that a broker cannot escape liability by delegating work to an employee, even a well-trained one. If an employee makes a material misstatement or misclassifies merchandise so as to understate duties, the broker of record can be held responsible for failing to exercise adequate supervision without personal knowledge of the specific error. The duty is one of active oversight rather than good intentions, so proof that the broker directed the conduct is not required. The chapter does not condition liability on the broker personally signing each affected entry.
  8. The correct answer is C (The chapter's restriction covers federal government employees, so her state job does not bar her.): The chapter explains that an individual license applicant must be at least twenty-one years old, be a United States citizen, and not be a federal government employee, subject to narrow exceptions. Her position with a state department of transportation is not federal employment, so that particular eligibility bar does not apply. She must still satisfy the age, citizenship, examination, and good moral character requirements before CBP grants a license. The chapter describes no requirement that a state employee obtain written permission from CBP headquarters.
  9. The correct answer is B (An individual license is not transferable by sale, gift, or any other arrangement.): The chapter states that an individual license belongs to the person and is not transferable to another person or entity by sale, gift, or otherwise. Passing the examination is personal to the candidate, so a shortened examination for the recipient is not a route the chapter describes. Because the daughter would not hold a license, letting her operate under her father's license number is an improper transfer rather than valid authority. CBP approval of a family gift transfer is not part of the licensing structure in the chapter.
  10. The correct answer is D (The grant must clearly identify the grantor and the grantee who receives the authority): A customs power of attorney must clearly identify the grantor and the grantee, and it must state the scope of authority granted. Using only a trade name without identifying the legal entity and the broker leaves the required parties unclear. The regulation does not require the importer number to appear twice, a broker countersignature, or tariff classifications in the grant. A document missing the identification element does not satisfy the execution requirements.
  11. The correct answer is B (An organization cannot sit for the examination, so the corporation needs a licensed individual.): The chapter explains that a corporation, association, or partnership may hold a broker license, but an organization cannot sit for an examination or exercise independent judgment on its own. Every entity license therefore depends on at least one individually licensed broker serving as an officer or partner and actively supervising the entity's operations. Registering the corporation to take the examination would not create that licensed oversight, and unlicensed staff cannot supply it. The chapter provides no entity examination fee or supervision plan that would cure the gap.
  12. The correct answer is D (She must file a timely status report confirming current employment and license status): The periodic status report confirms a broker's current employment and license status so CBP knows who is actively practicing under a license and where. A broker who fails to timely file the report can have the license placed in an inactive or suspended posture, separate from any disciplinary action for misconduct. She does not need to surrender her individual license or obtain a power of attorney from an employer. A national permit is not tied to each state where the broker works.
  13. The correct answer is A (It is a recognized ground for a monetary penalty, suspension, or license revocation): Under 19 USC 1641(d), CBP may impose a monetary penalty, suspend, or revoke a broker's license on several enumerated grounds. A felony conviction, particularly one involving fraud or dishonesty, is an enumerated ground for license suspension or revocation under 19 USC 1641(d), independent of whether the conduct occurred during customs business. Broker discipline is a CBP regulatory matter distinct from, and in addition to, any private liability the conduct might create. Because the conduct involves the individual's own licensed status, it can affect that individual's license directly, not only a separate corporate permit.
  14. The correct answer is C (He can be held responsible for failing to exercise adequate supervision of the work): A broker cannot escape liability by delegating work to an employee, even a well-trained one, and the broker of record can be held responsible for failing to exercise adequate supervision. The duty is one of active oversight, so CBP does not have to prove the broker knew about the specific error. Delegating classification decisions with no sign-off or review process falls short of the internal controls the chapter describes. A client complaint is not a precondition for CBP to pursue a supervision violation.
  15. The correct answer is A (The former employee is transacting unlicensed customs business and faces a civil monetary penalty): Once authority is revoked and CBP is notified, continuing to file entries in the brokerage's name is unlicensed customs business. Under 19 USC 1641(b)(6) and 19 CFR 111.4, that conduct carries a civil monetary penalty of up to 10,000 dollars per transaction, assessed under the procedures in 19 CFR Part 111 Subpart E, and it is not a crime. Permit renewal timing is unrelated to whether the individual's authority to act was terminated, and prior legitimate access does not immunize conduct that continues after authority is withdrawn.
  16. The correct answer is B (The firm is entitled to notice of the charges and an opportunity to respond): Before CBP can suspend or revoke a license or permit, or assess a monetary penalty above a certain threshold, the broker is entitled to notice of the charges and an opportunity to respond. For serious matters, that process includes referral to an administrative law judge for a hearing, with further appeal rights afterward. CBP cannot impose the remedy without following the statutory procedure. A jury trial or broker-selected arbitration panel is not the process the chapter describes.
  17. The correct answer is A (The power of attorney must be executed by someone authorized to bind the corporation): A corporate power of attorney must be signed by an officer or other individual actually authorized to bind the corporation, and the broker should be able to show that authority if CBP questions it. A warehouse supervisor with no corporate office or delegated signing authority generally cannot bind the company this way. CBP can treat entries made under a defective power of attorney as unauthorized, exposing both the importer and the broker to liability. Notarization by the broker itself is not a substitute for proper corporate authority.
  18. The correct answer is D (The judge conducts a hearing, and further appeal rights follow the decision): For serious matters, the statutory procedure includes referral to an administrative law judge for a hearing, with further appeal rights afterward. The hearing is an administrative proceeding, not a criminal sentencing, and the judge can receive evidence from the parties. CBP's role does not end when the judge rules, because the decision is subject to appeal. The chapter describes these procedural protections as mandatory before CBP can suspend or revoke a license or permit.
  19. The correct answer is A (It is a recognized ground for a monetary penalty, suspension, or license revocation): Under 19 USC 1641(d), CBP may impose a monetary penalty, suspend, or revoke a broker's license on several enumerated grounds. Knowing, intentional fraud in customs transactions is among the most serious grounds for revocation under 19 USC 1641(d) and can also expose the broker to separate customs penalties. Broker discipline is a CBP regulatory matter distinct from, and in addition to, any private liability the conduct might create. Because the conduct involves the individual's own licensed status, it can affect that individual's license directly, not only a separate corporate permit.
  20. The correct answer is B (Yes, inadequate supervision is an independent ground even when no loss results): Failure to provide responsible supervision and control is itself an independent, separately actionable ground for discipline under 19 CFR Part 111 and 19 USC 1641. CBP does not need to prove that a bad outcome occurred, because inadequate internal controls are enough. Written procedures, clear lines of authority, filing review, training, and error correction are the controls CBP expects to see. A monetary loss is not a precondition for discipline, and no prior warning is required before CBP acts on a supervision failure.
  21. The correct answer is A (The former employee is transacting unlicensed customs business and faces a civil monetary penalty): Once authority is revoked and CBP is notified, continuing to file entries in the brokerage's name is unlicensed customs business. Under 19 USC 1641(b)(6) and 19 CFR 111.4, that conduct carries a civil monetary penalty of up to 10,000 dollars per transaction, assessed under the procedures in 19 CFR Part 111 Subpart E, and it is not a crime. Permit renewal timing is unrelated to whether the individual's authority to act was terminated, and prior legitimate access does not immunize conduct that continues after authority is withdrawn.
  22. The correct answer is D (A false or misleading statement to CBP made while transacting customs business): The chapter lists making a false or misleading statement to CBP, whether in a license application, a status report, or in transacting customs business such as on an entry or protest, as a ground for discipline. Rosalind's knowingly incorrect origin statement on an entry falls directly within that ground. Recordkeeping failures and improper license transfers are separate grounds that this conduct does not describe. The bond amount does not determine whether the statement itself is a disciplinary violation.
  23. The correct answer is C (The individually licensed brokers, because continuing education is tied to each active license): Individually licensed brokers must complete continuing education as a condition of maintaining an active license. The obligation attaches to each licensed individual, so all four supervisory license holders must be tracked, not only the qualifying officer. Unlicensed entry writers do not hold licenses and therefore do not carry the individual continuing education requirement. Missing the requirement can place a license in an inactive or suspended posture.
  24. The correct answer is A (It is a recognized ground for a monetary penalty, suspension, or license revocation): Under 19 USC 1641(d), CBP may impose a monetary penalty, suspend, or revoke a broker's license on several enumerated grounds. Sustained failure of responsible supervision and control is itself a statutory basis for a monetary penalty, suspension, or revocation under 19 USC 1641(d), separate from any individual entry error. Broker discipline is a CBP regulatory matter distinct from, and in addition to, any private liability the conduct might create. Because the conduct involves the individual's own licensed status, it can affect that individual's license directly, not only a separate corporate permit.
  25. The correct answer is D (The schedule violates the rules because broker records generally must be kept for five years): Broker records relating to customs business generally must be retained for five years from the date of entry, and records not tied to a specific entry are kept five years from creation or receipt. Destroying entry files at three years and correspondence at four years would cut both periods short. Electronic conversion does not shorten the retention period, though electronic recordkeeping is permitted when the system meets CBP standards for accuracy, retrievability, and integrity. A broker who destroys required records before the period runs violates the recordkeeping requirements and can face discipline for that alone.
  26. The correct answer is B (The requirement that records be kept readily accessible and producible on reasonable request): Required broker records must be kept readily accessible and producible on reasonable request, and electronic recordkeeping is permitted only when the system meets CBP standards for accuracy, retrievability, and integrity. A system the broker cannot access fails the retrievability expectation. The chapter does not require records to remain inside the United States, to be duplicated in a second format, or to be reported to CBP before offsite storage. Losing access to two years of entry data is a recordkeeping failure.
  27. The correct answer is A (Yes, if the conditions set out in 19 CFR 111.36(c) are satisfied): 19 CFR 111.36(b) bars an agreement that lets fees from customs business inure to an unlicensed person, but 19 CFR 111.36(c) allows a broker to compensate a freight forwarder for referring brokerage business when the conditions are met. The importer must be notified in advance of the name of the broker chosen for the work, the broker must transmit its brokerage charges or a statement of them to the importer, a customs power of attorney must be executed directly with the importer rather than through the forwarder, and the agreement cannot restrict direct communication between the importer and the broker. Written consent alone is not the test, and the forwarder's corporate form does not make the arrangement unlawful.
  28. The correct answer is C (Five years from the date each record was created or received by the brokerage): For records not tied to a specific entry, the retention period runs five years from the date the record was created or received. Records that document a particular entry are retained five years from the date of entry instead. The ten-year and three-year periods in the other options do not appear in the chapter's recordkeeping rules. This obligation runs independently against the broker and cannot be shifted to the importer.
  29. The correct answer is A (A civil monetary penalty of up to 10,000 dollars per transaction under 19 USC 1641): Unlicensed practice of customs brokerage is addressed by 19 USC 1641(b)(6), which provides a civil monetary penalty of up to 10,000 dollars per transaction and is implemented at 19 CFR 111.4 with the penalty procedures in 19 CFR Part 111 Subpart E. The conduct is not a crime and carries no imprisonment. A later review by a licensed broker does not retroactively legitimize the earlier unlicensed conduct, and the unlicensed individual, not only the client, bears direct exposure.
  30. The correct answer is C (No customs broker's bond is required, because brokerage work uses the importer's entry bond): There is no customs broker's bond requirement of any kind. The importer's continuous bond secures the importer's own obligations on its entries, and the brokerage holds no bond of its own for those entries. A brokerage's obligation to handle duties responsibly does not create a separate bond requirement, and the number of offices a broker maintains has no bearing on bond obligations either.
  31. The correct answer is D (The document can be valid if a properly drafted grant covers the same essential elements): CBP Form 5291 is the standard form for a customs power of attorney, but a properly drafted document covering the same essential elements is also acceptable. The essential elements include identification of the grantor and grantee and a statement of the scope of authority granted. A CBP officer need not witness the signature, and the regulation does not require filing a copy with a port director. The brokerage's own form therefore can serve as a valid grant.
  32. The correct answer is B (No customs broker's bond is required, and the firm holds no bond for its own account): There is no customs broker's bond requirement, and no such bond appears in 19 CFR Part 111, 19 USC 1641, or 19 CFR Part 113. The bonds that appear in brokerage work are the importer's entry bond, which secures duties, taxes, and fees on an entry, and the carrier, custodial, and foreign trade zone operator bonds held by those other parties. Holding a license does not trigger any bond obligation for the brokerage itself, and there is no grace period or waiver to wait for.
  33. The correct answer is A (The former employee is transacting unlicensed customs business and faces a civil monetary penalty): Once authority is revoked and CBP is notified, continuing to file entries in the brokerage's name is unlicensed customs business. Under 19 USC 1641(b)(6) and 19 CFR 111.4, that conduct carries a civil monetary penalty of up to 10,000 dollars per transaction, assessed under the procedures in 19 CFR Part 111 Subpart E, and it is not a crime. Permit renewal timing is unrelated to whether the individual's authority to act was terminated, and prior legitimate access does not immunize conduct that continues after authority is withdrawn.
  34. The correct answer is C (The protest may fall outside the scope of authority that the document actually granted): A customs power of attorney must state the scope of authority granted, which typically covers making, signing, and filing entries, paying and receiving monies, and executing related documents such as protests and drawback claims. Because Cordelia's document addresses entries only and is silent on protests, the brokerage's authority to file the protest is questionable. A grant can be limited to particular activities, and the regulation does not imply protest authority into a narrower grant. The brokerage should obtain authorization covering protests before acting.
  35. The correct answer is B (The grant must be executed by the proprietor of the importing business or an authorized agent): A customs power of attorney must be executed by a person with actual authority to bind the principal, which for a sole proprietorship means the proprietor or an authorized agent. A senior entry clerk employed by the brokerage does not hold authority to bind the importing client. The regulation does not require two independent witnesses or execution by the receiving broker. Authority to bind the principal is the controlling element.
  36. The correct answer is C (It exceeded the two-year maximum period that applies to a partnership grantee): Under 19 CFR 141.32, a power of attorney granted to a partnership must be limited to a period not exceeding two years from execution, though it may be renewed. That fixed renewal period is specific to partnership grantees and is commonly tested because individual and corporate brokers are not subject to it. A power of attorney given to an individual broker is generally unlimited in duration unless it specifies an expiration date or is revoked. The failure to renew this partnership grant leaves the brokerage without current authority.
  37. The correct answer is B (The brokerage transacted customs business without authorization after the grant was revoked): A power of attorney remains valid until it is properly revoked, and a principal may revoke it affirmatively in writing. A broker who continues filing entries under a power of attorney that the broker knows or should know has been revoked is transacting customs business without authorization. That is a compliance violation separate from the quality of the entry work itself. The regulation does not require the importer to file the revocation with a port director before it takes effect.
  38. The correct answer is A (It is a recognized ground for a monetary penalty, suspension, or license revocation): Under 19 USC 1641(d), CBP may impose a monetary penalty, suspend, or revoke a broker's license on several enumerated grounds. This is a recognized ground for suspension, revocation, or a monetary penalty under 19 USC 1641(d) because CBP relies on the accuracy of filings connected to a broker's licensure and conduct. Broker discipline is a CBP regulatory matter distinct from, and in addition to, any private liability the conduct might create. Because the conduct involves the individual's own licensed status, it can affect that individual's license directly, not only a separate corporate permit.
  39. The correct answer is C (Promptly designating a licensed officer who is actively engaged in supervising operations): A corporate license requires an individually licensed officer who is actively engaged in supervising the entity's brokerage operations. When that officer leaves, the company must promptly designate a licensed replacement rather than promote unlicensed staff into the role. Adding entry writers or transferring the license to an unlicensed manager does not satisfy the qualifying officer requirement. A license is not transferable to an unlicensed person, and the company needs an active licensed officer supervising its work.
  40. The correct answer is B (The corporation must provide licensed oversight of all work done on its behalf): A national permit issued under 19 CFR 111.19 lets a broker transact customs business throughout the customs territory, but the broker remains responsible for exercising responsible supervision and control over every person who conducts customs business on its behalf. Unlicensed branch managers filing entries daily without licensed oversight cannot satisfy that duty. Nothing in the chapter limits a corporation to one office or requires two licensed brokers at each branch. Requiring branch managers to pass the examination would not by itself satisfy the licensed oversight requirement.
  41. The correct answer is C (A federal government employee generally may not hold an individual broker license under the regulation): 19 CFR Part 111 requires an individual license applicant to be at least twenty-one, a United States citizen, of good moral character, and not a federal government employee, with only narrow exceptions. Working for the Department of Homeland Security places this applicant squarely inside the general prohibition. The timing of his brokerage work does not change the employment status that the regulation addresses. Passing the examination does not overcome this separate eligibility bar.
  42. The correct answer is A (The broker failed to retain the power of attorney as a required business record): A power of attorney authorizing the broker to act is part of the broker's customs business records and must be retained and made available to CBP on request, just like entry documentation. Powers of attorney are not carved out of the general recordkeeping obligation. The broker, not only the importer, is independently responsible for keeping its own copy on file. Losing the document is a recordkeeping violation, not merely an administrative fee problem, and can expose the broker to a penalty.
  43. The correct answer is B (Passing the examination is a threshold requirement, and CBP still reviews character and fitness): Earning a score of seventy-five percent or higher satisfies one requirement, but it does not by itself entitle a candidate to a license. CBP evaluates good moral character through a background investigation before granting the license. The outcome of that review can prevent licensure even after a passing examination score. A corporate license involves separate entity requirements but does not remove the individual character review.
  44. The correct answer is C (The brokerage is failing to exercise responsible supervision and control over its customs business): Responsible supervision and control requires a licensed broker to review and be accountable for the substantive customs decisions made in the firm's name, not merely to sign paperwork after the fact. Letting an unlicensed employee independently classify goods and file entries without meaningful broker review is a textbook failure of that duty. CBP treats inadequate supervision as grounds for a corrective action letter, monetary penalty, or suspension of the broker's license. The clerk's lack of a license does not shield the brokerage from responsibility for how its business is actually run.
  45. The correct answer is A (The revocation is effective against the broker as soon as the broker actually receives it): A power of attorney remains valid until the grantor revokes it, and revocation is effective against the broker once the broker has actual notice of it, not on some later administrative or accounting date. CBP does not need to log the revocation for it to bind the broker's future conduct. Entries the broker files after receiving the revocation letter without a new authorization would be unauthorized. The date the importer wrote on the letter is irrelevant if the broker had not yet received it.

  48. The correct answer is C (The license may be suspended or revoked for failing to file the triennial status report): Every licensed broker must file a periodic status report with CBP, commonly called the triennial report, confirming current contact information and continued eligibility to hold the license. Failing to file within the required window can result in suspension or revocation of the license without a hearing, because the filing is treated as a condition of continued licensure. This is not a discretionary or optional filing. Missing the deadline does not convert the license type or require retaking the exam, it puts the existing license itself at risk.
  49.   50. The correct answer is D (The brokerage should obtain a national permit, which authorizes customs business throughout the United States): Under the modernized broker regulations, a national permit authorizes a broker to conduct customs business throughout the United States rather than requiring a separate permit at every individual district or port. This replaced the older port-by-port district permit structure for most purposes. Expanding geographic operations does not require an entirely new broker license, it is a permitting matter. Bond amount by itself does not confer geographic authority to operate nationally.
