Because the conduct involves the individual's own licensed status, it can affect that individual's license directly, not only a separate corporate permit.
  53. The correct answer is D (The corporation or partnership must separately obtain its own broker's license naming a qualifying officer): An individual broker's license belongs to that individual and does not automatically cover a corporation, partnership, or association the individual later forms, so the entity must apply separately for its own broker's license naming a qualifying officer. Personal signature by the individual on every entry does not substitute for the entity holding its own license. Filing articles of incorporation with a state agency has no bearing on CBP licensure. Until the entity's own license issues, conducting customs business in the entity's name would exceed the individual's personal license.
  54. The correct answer is B (It is a recognized ground for a monetary penalty, suspension, or license revocation): Under 19 USC 1641(d), CBP may impose a monetary penalty, suspend, or revoke a broker's license on several enumerated grounds. Knowing, intentional fraud in customs transactions is among the most serious grounds for revocation under 19 USC 1641(d) and can also expose the broker to separate customs penalties. Broker discipline is a CBP regulatory matter distinct from, and in addition to, any private liability the conduct might create. Because the conduct involves the individual's own licensed status, it can affect that individual's license directly, not only a separate corporate permit.
  55. The correct answer is D (It is a recognized ground for a monetary penalty, suspension, or license revocation): Under 19 USC 1641(d), CBP may impose a monetary penalty, suspend, or revoke a broker's license on several enumerated grounds. Sustained failure of responsible supervision and control is itself a statutory basis for a monetary penalty, suspension, or revocation under 19 USC 1641(d), separate from any individual entry error. Broker discipline is a CBP regulatory matter distinct from, and in addition to, any private liability the conduct might create. Because the conduct involves the individual's own licensed status, it can affect that individual's license directly, not only a separate corporate permit.
  56. The correct answer is C (The qualifying officer must be a licensed broker responsible for the corporation's customs business): A corporation, partnership, or association may not receive a broker's license unless at least one officer, partner, or member holds an individual broker's license and exercises responsible supervision and control over the entity's customs business. That qualifying officer is the link between the individual license and the organizational license under 19 CFR Part 111. CBP can revoke or suspend the corporate license if the qualifying officer ceases to actively supervise the brokerage operations. Ownership percentage and bar admission are irrelevant to who may serve as a qualifying officer.
  57. The correct answer is A (Yes, if the conditions set out in 19 CFR 111.36(c) are satisfied): 19 CFR 111.36(b) bars an agreement that lets fees from customs business inure to an unlicensed person, but 19 CFR 111.36(c) allows a broker to compensate a freight forwarder for referring brokerage business when the conditions are met. The importer must be notified in advance of the name of the broker chosen for the work, the broker must transmit its brokerage charges or a statement of them to the importer, a customs power of attorney must be executed directly with the importer rather than through the forwarder, and the agreement cannot restrict direct communication between the importer and the broker. Written consent alone is not the test, and the forwarder's corporate form does not make the arrangement unlawful.
  58. The correct answer is B (CBP may assess a monetary penalty as an alternative to suspension, capped per violation): Section 1641(d) gives CBP the authority to assess a monetary penalty against a broker as its own remedy, separate from and not necessarily following suspension or revocation, subject to a statutory per-violation maximum. The penalty amount is not unlimited or purely discretionary, the statute caps it per violation. Revocation is not a prerequisite to a monetary penalty, the two are independent enforcement tools CBP may choose among. The statute applies to individual brokers as well as corporate and partnership brokers.
  59. The correct answer is A (A civil monetary penalty of up to 10,000 dollars per transaction under 19 USC 1641): Unlicensed practice of customs brokerage is addressed by 19 USC 1641(b)(6), which provides a civil monetary penalty of up to 10,000 dollars per transaction and is implemented at 19 CFR 111.4 with the penalty procedures in 19 CFR Part 111 Subpart E. The conduct is not a crime and carries no imprisonment. A later review by a licensed broker does not retroactively legitimize the earlier unlicensed conduct, and the unlicensed individual, not only the client, bears direct exposure.
  60. The correct answer is C (The broker failed to retain the power of attorney as a required business record): A power of attorney authorizing the broker to act is part of the broker's customs business records and must be retained and made available to CBP on request, just like entry documentation. Powers of attorney are not carved out of the general recordkeeping obligation. The broker, not only the importer, is independently responsible for keeping its own copy on file. Losing the document is a recordkeeping violation, not merely an administrative fee problem, and can expose the broker to a penalty.
  61. The correct answer is D (CBP may assess a monetary penalty as an alternative to suspension, capped per violation): Section 1641(d) gives CBP the authority to assess a monetary penalty against a broker as its own remedy, separate from and not necessarily following suspension or revocation, subject to a statutory per-violation maximum. The penalty amount is not unlimited or purely discretionary, the statute caps it per violation. Revocation is not a prerequisite to a monetary penalty, the two are independent enforcement tools CBP may choose among. The statute applies to individual brokers as well as corporate and partnership brokers.
  62. The correct answer is C (The broker is entitled to notice and a hearing before an administrative law judge): Before a license is revoked or suspended for cause on a contested basis, the broker is generally entitled to written notice of the charges and an opportunity for a hearing, which for revocation and suspension proceedings is conducted before an administrative law judge. This due process step exists precisely because revocation ends the broker's ability to conduct customs business. Judicial review by the Court of International Trade is available after the agency process, not as a substitute for it. The length of time the broker has held the license does not determine hearing eligibility.
  63. The correct answer is A (The former employee is transacting unlicensed customs business and faces a civil monetary penalty): Once authority is revoked and CBP is notified, continuing to file entries in the brokerage's name is unlicensed customs business. Under 19 USC 1641(b)(6) and 19 CFR 111.4, that conduct carries a civil monetary penalty of up to 10,000 dollars per transaction, assessed under the procedures in 19 CFR Part 111 Subpart E, and it is not a crime. Permit renewal timing is unrelated to whether the individual's authority to act was terminated, and prior legitimate access does not immunize conduct that continues after authority is withdrawn.
  64. The correct answer is B (No customs broker's bond requirement exists, so the firm holds no bond of its own): There is no customs broker's bond requirement, and no such bond appears in 19 CFR Part 111, 19 USC 1641, or 19 CFR Part 113. The bonds that appear in brokerage work are the importer's entry bond, which secures duties, taxes, and fees on an entry, and the carrier, custodial, and foreign trade zone operator bonds held by those other parties. A brokerage therefore has no bond of its own to obtain, and the other options describe obligations that do not exist under the broker regulations.
  65. The correct answer is C (The brokerage should obtain a national permit, which authorizes customs business throughout the United States): Under the modernized broker regulations, a national permit authorizes a broker to conduct customs business throughout the United States rather than requiring a separate permit at every individual district or port. This replaced the older port-by-port district permit structure for most purposes. Expanding geographic operations does not require an entirely new broker license, it is a permitting matter. Bond amount by itself does not confer geographic authority to operate nationally.
  66. The correct answer is A (It is a recognized ground for a monetary penalty, suspension, or license revocation): Under 19 USC 1641(d), CBP may impose a monetary penalty, suspend, or revoke a broker's license on several enumerated grounds. This is a recognized ground for suspension, revocation, or a monetary penalty under 19 USC 1641(d) because CBP relies on the accuracy of filings connected to a broker's licensure and conduct. Broker discipline is a CBP regulatory matter distinct from, and in addition to, any private liability the conduct might create. Because the conduct involves the individual's own licensed status, it can affect that individual's license directly, not only a separate corporate permit.
  67. Broker discipline is a CBP regulatory matter distinct from, and in addition to, any private liability the conduct might create. Because the conduct involves the individual's own licensed status, it can affect that individual's license directly, not only a separate corporate permit.
  68. The correct answer is B (The power of attorney from a dissolved entity may no longer grant valid authority): A power of attorney is authority granted by a legal person, and when that grantor ceases to legally exist the authority underlying the power of attorney is called into question, so continuing to file entries on the strength of a stale power of attorney creates real exposure for the broker. The authority does not simply survive dissolution indefinitely regardless of the facts. Filing one renewal form does not retroactively cure entries already made without valid authority. This exposure is not limited to any one type of business entity, it can arise whether the grantor was a corporation, partnership, or other entity.
  69. The correct answer is A (It is a recognized ground for a monetary penalty, suspension, or license revocation): Under 19 USC 1641(d), CBP may impose a monetary penalty, suspend, or revoke a broker's license on several enumerated grounds. Conducting customs business outside the scope of one's own license or permit is treated as unauthorized practice and supports discipline under 19 USC 1641(d). Broker discipline is a CBP regulatory matter distinct from, and in addition to, any private liability the conduct might create. Because the conduct involves the individual's own licensed status, it can affect that individual's license directly, not only a separate corporate permit.
  70. The correct answer is B (The broker is entitled to notice and a hearing before an administrative law judge): Before a license is revoked or suspended for cause on a contested basis, the broker is generally entitled to written notice of the charges and an opportunity for a hearing, which for revocation and suspension proceedings is conducted before an administrative law judge. This due process step exists precisely because revocation ends the broker's ability to conduct customs business. Judicial review by the Court of International Trade is available after the agency process, not as a substitute for it. The length of time the broker has held the license does not determine hearing eligibility.
  71. The correct answer is C (A broker must promptly notify CBP of address changes so official notices reach the broker): Licensed brokers are required to keep CBP informed of current business information, including office address, so that official notices and communications actually reach the broker. Failing to do so for an extended period, especially while CBP correspondence goes unanswered, can itself become a compliance problem separate from whatever the original mail concerned. CBP does not rely on client filings to track a broker's own contact information. An unchanged phone number does not cure the failure to update the address CBP has on file, and repeated unreachability can factor into license status.

  75. The correct answer is D (The license may be suspended or revoked for failing to file the triennial status report): Every licensed broker must file a periodic status report with CBP, commonly called the triennial report, confirming current contact information and continued eligibility to hold the license. Failing to file within the required window can result in suspension or revocation of the license without a hearing, because the filing is treated as a condition of continued licensure. This is not a discretionary or optional filing. Missing the deadline does not convert the license type or require retaking the exam, it puts the existing license itself at risk.
  76. The correct answer is B (The revocation is effective against the broker as soon as the broker actually receives it): A power of attorney remains valid until the grantor revokes it, and revocation is effective against the broker once the broker has actual notice of it, not on some later administrative or accounting date. CBP does not need to log the revocation for it to bind the broker's future conduct. Entries the broker files after receiving the revocation letter without a new authorization would be unauthorized. The date the importer wrote on the letter is irrelevant if the broker had not yet received it.

There is no blanket requirement that original paper be kept once a compliant electronic system is in place. CBP does not need to pre-clear each scanned document individually before the paper original may be discarded. The rule applies to records generally and is not limited to entries transmitted through a particular filing interface.

The $1,400.00 paid to the packing firm for export preparation fits that description. Paying a third party rather than the seller does not remove the cost from the addition. The packing cost increases transaction value.

  19. The correct answer is A (Dutiable value generally falls because the middleman's markup is stripped out): the first sale rule allows the earlier factory price to serve as transaction value when the three conditions are documented, rather than the higher price the United States importer paid the middleman. Using that earlier price generally produces a lower dutiable value because the middleman's markup is removed from the figure. Two documented sales do not raise the value, and the relationship status of the middleman is not one of the conditions. The importer must support the first sale claim with records.
  20. The correct answer is D (Yes, failing to produce records and failing supervision are independent grounds for discipline): Section 1641(d) lists several independent grounds for license discipline, including failure to exercise responsible supervision and control and failure to comply with CBP's recordkeeping and production requirements, none of which require a criminal conviction first. CBP's civil licensing authority operates separately from the criminal justice system. A client complaint can trigger scrutiny but is not a legal precondition to CBP acting on conduct it identifies through its own oversight, such as an audit or records request. The absence of criminal charges does not limit CBP to a mere warning when the statutory grounds for suspension or revocation are met.
  21. The correct answer is B (They are added to transaction value once the amount can be determined): 19 USC 1401a(b)(1) adds the proceeds of any subsequent resale, disposal, or use of the imported merchandise that accrue directly or indirectly to the seller. The five percent share is a seller's interest in the buyer's United States resales and can be calculated from the resale records. The timing of the payment after importation does not exclude it from the addition. The amount enters transaction value once it can be determined.

  23. The correct answer is D (The brokerage must still produce the records because the standard retention period is generally five years): Customs brokers must generally retain records relating to their customs transactions for five years from the date of entry, and that duty does not disappear because the records were moved off-site. A request made within the five-year window must be honored regardless of where the broker chose to store the files. Both the importer and the broker independently carry recordkeeping obligations, so the broker cannot point to the importer to avoid producing its own records. Failing to produce required records on request can itself expose the broker to a penalty.
  24. The correct answer is B ($917.00, the two components calculated and added together): a compound rate requires both components to be computed on one tariff line. The specific component is 3,500 kilograms multiplied by $0.15 per kilogram, which equals $525.00, and the ad valorem component is $9,800.00 multiplied by 4.0 percent, which equals $392.00. Adding $525.00 and $392.00 produces $917.00. The other figures report only one component or multiply the rate figures together.
  25. The correct answer is B (A bona fide sale, clear destination for the United States, and an arm's length price): the first sale rule permits transaction value to be taken from an earlier sale in a multi-tiered chain only when that earlier sale was a bona fide sale, the goods were clearly destined for exportation to the United States at the time of that sale, and the sale was conducted at arm's length, without any non-market influence on the price. The middleman's profit, a below cost factory price, or a resale deadline does not satisfy those conditions. The importer must document all three elements. Without that documentation CBP uses the later sale price.

  27. The correct answer is D (The entered value is multiplied by the rate percentage): ad valorem duty is calculated as the entered or appraised value multiplied by the rate, so $48,000.00 multiplied by 3.5 percent produces the duty. The rate structure is a percentage of value rather than a fixed charge per unit. Dividing the value by the rate or averaging the two figures does not produce the statutory duty. The broker applies the percentage to the established value.
  28. The correct answer is D ($405.00, the quantity of 900 dozen pairs multiplied by $0.45): a specific duty is a flat amount per unit of quantity, so the calculation uses quantity rather than value. Nine hundred dozen pairs multiplied by $0.45 per dozen pair equals $405.00. The value of the gloves does not enter the calculation under a specific rate. The other figures apply the rate to value or divide the rate instead of multiplying.
  29. The correct answer is C (Additions rest on objective, quantifiable data and are not already included in the price): 19 USC 1401a(b)(1) adds the five statutory items only to the extent they are based on objective, quantifiable data and are not already reflected in the price. This commission cannot be separated from other charges and has no supporting record, so no figure can be quantified. The seller's request for payment, or the mere connection of a cost to the goods, does not supply the missing data. The broker cannot add an unquantified amount.
  30. The correct answer is A (Related, because both parties are controlled by a third party): 19 USC 1401a(g) treats parties as related when they are family members, officers or directors of one another's businesses, business partners, employer and employee, a person owning, controlling, or holding the power to vote five percent or more of an organization's outstanding voting stock and that organization, one party controlling the other, or both controlled by a third party. The investment group controlled both the seller and the buyer through board appointments and operating budgets. Stock ownership is not required for the controlled by a third party category. The parties are therefore related.
  31. The correct answer is C (Both the ad valorem and the specific components are calculated and added): a compound duty combines an ad valorem component and a specific component on the same tariff line, and the total duty is the sum of the two calculated amounts. For this entry, the ad valorem percentage applies to the appraised value and the per liter amount applies to the entered quantity. Using only one component understates the duty, and choosing the higher component is not the statutory method. Both components must be computed and added.
  32. The correct answer is D (The rate applicable to the specific kind of transaction being converted): Under 19 CFR 159.36, where more than one rate is certified for a date, the rate applicable to the specific kind of transaction at issue is used. The rule avoids selecting a rate certified for a different category of transaction. The highest rate, an average of the rates, and a rate requested by the importer are not authorized. The broker must match the rate to the transaction type.
  33. The correct answer is B (The importer, which must show the relationship did not influence the price): 19 USC 1401a(b)(2) makes transaction value unavailable in a related party sale when the relationship influenced the price, unless the importer shows otherwise. That language places the responsibility on the importer, which has access to the pricing and cost information needed to make the showing. The foreign seller, CBP, and the broker do not carry that burden. The entry can proceed on transaction value only after the importer supplies the support.
  34. The correct answer is D (The certified rate of exchange published by the Federal Reserve Bank of New York): 19 CFR 159.31 through 159.36 require conversion of a foreign currency invoice into United States dollars using the certified rate of exchange published by the Federal Reserve Bank of New York for the relevant date. A central bank rate, a commercial wiring rate, or a tariff publication rate is not the rate those regulations use. The certified rate controls the conversion. The broker must apply it before calculating duty.
  35. The correct answer is A ($2,000.00, the tool value apportioned across the 500 units imported): the assist value of $12,000.00 covers a planned production run of 3,000 units, so the apportioned amount per unit is $4.00. The first shipment contains 500 units, and 500 multiplied by $4.00 equals $2,000.00. That is the amount added to this shipment's transaction value. Charging the full tool value or an arbitrary fraction to the first entry would not apportion the assist consistently.
  36. The correct answer is B (No, because the holder is related to each corporation, not the two to each other): Under 19 USC 1401a(g)(1)(F) and 19 CFR 152.102(g)(6), a person who directly or indirectly owns, controls, or holds the power to vote five percent or more of an organization's outstanding voting stock is related to that organization. The holding company is therefore related to the buyer and to the seller, but that relationship does not make the buyer and seller related to each other, because they share no other connection. A fifty percent threshold does not appear in the statute, and common ownership by a third party is not the same as common control. The related-party analysis then governs whether transaction value can be used for the sale.
  37. The correct answer is D ($43,500.00 of deductive value after subtracting the value added by processing): when merchandise is sold in the United States only after further processing, 19 USC 1401a(d) allows an additional deduction for the value added by that processing. Starting from the $90,000.00 resale price, the broker subtracts $13,500.00 for usual profit and general expenses, $2,000.00 for post-importation costs, $6,000.00 for duties, and $25,000.00 for the processing. Those subtractions produce $43,500.00, which is the deductive value of the goods. Omitting the processing deduction would instead leave $68,500.00.
  38. The correct answer is B (It is added because it relates to the merchandise and is a condition of sale): 19 USC 1401a(b)(1) adds royalties or license fees related to the imported merchandise that the buyer must pay, directly or indirectly, as a condition of the sale for exportation to the United States. The patented circuit is embodied in the radios, so the royalty relates to the merchandise being valued. The buyer could not complete the purchase without paying it. The $4,500.00 must be added to the $60,000.00 price.
  39. The correct answer is C (It is deducted as the usual cost of post-importation transportation and insurance): 19 USC 1401a(d) deducts the usual costs of transportation and insurance incurred after importation when deductive value is computed. The $2,800.00 moved the goods from the port to the regional warehouse, so it is a post-importation charge. The amount is not added to the resale price and is not a pre-importation freight charge. Further processing is a separate deductive value variant.
  40. The correct answer is B (The die value is apportioned reasonably and consistently across the anticipated production run): 19 USC 1401a(h)(1) requires the value of an assist to be apportioned reasonably, commonly across the first shipment or the entire anticipated production run, and applied consistently. The die will serve a planned run of 6,000 units imported in several shipments. Charging the whole die value to the first shipment would distort that shipment's value. The chosen method must be applied consistently to the remaining shipments.
  41. The correct answer is C (The greatest aggregate quantity unit price for United States sales to unrelated buyers near importation): 19 USC 1401a(d) starts deductive value from the unit price at which the greatest aggregate quantity of the imported merchandise, or of identical or similar merchandise, is sold in the United States at or about the time of importation, in its imported condition, to unrelated buyers. Working backward from that resale price produces deductible value. Producer costs and foreign domestic prices belong to other methods. The greatest aggregate quantity resale price is the statutory starting point.
  42. The correct answer is B (An assist because tools, dies, and molds used in production are listed categories): 19 USC 1401a(h)(1) lists tools, dies, and molds used in producing the imported merchandise among the items that count as assists when the buyer supplies them free of charge or at reduced cost. The molds were lent at no charge and used only to make the imported housings. Their measurable value must be apportioned and added. A packing cost, a selling expense, or a royalty describes a different category.
  43. The correct answer is D (Yes, because they are officers or directors of one another's businesses): 19 USC 1401a(g) lists officers or directors of one another's businesses among the related party categories. The importer's president sits on the manufacturer's board, and the manufacturer's president sits on the importer's board. No stock ownership is required for that category. The parties are related for purposes of the valuation analysis.
  44. The correct answer is A (It does not block transaction value because the restriction is imposed by law): 19 USC 1401a(b)(2) lists the restrictions that defeat transaction value, and it carves out restrictions imposed by law. The permit requirement comes from federal law, not from a private agreement between the parties. The unrelated status of the buyer and seller is not the reason the restriction is tolerated. The statutory exception for legally imposed restrictions controls the analysis.
  45. The correct answer is C (Employer and employee, which is a listed related party category): 19 USC 1401a(g) includes employer and employee among the relationships that make parties related. The consultancy contract places the seller's export manager on the buyer's payroll for the same period as the sale. That arrangement implicates the employer and employee category. Other listed categories, such as family members, business partners, and shared stock ownership, are not shown by these facts.
  46. The correct answer is D (It is not added because the value is already included in the price): each statutory addition under 19 USC 1401a(b)(1) applies only to the extent the amount is not already included in the price actually paid or payable. The seller's invoice price already contains the $9,000.00 tooling charge, so the buyer effectively paid for the tool through the price. Adding the tool value again would count the same amount twice. The broker should leave transaction value at the invoiced figure for this item.
  47. The correct answer is B ($52,000.00, because the total payment made to or for the seller controls): the price actually paid or payable is the total amount the buyer paid, or will pay, to or for the benefit of the seller for the merchandise. 19 USC 1401a(b) looks at the substance of the payment rather than the label placed on it. The records show the $4,000.00 described as a documentation fee was part of what the buyer paid to obtain the pumps. Splitting the amount on the invoice does not reduce the price actually paid or payable.
  48. The correct answer is C (They are excluded because engineering performed within the United States is not an assist): 19 USC 1401a(h)(1) covers engineering, development, artwork, design work, and plans undertaken outside the United States. Work performed within the United States is expressly left out of the assist definition. The Chicago team developed the drawings domestically, so the cost is not added even though the buyer supplied the drawings free of charge. A foreign firm repeating the work would not make the domestic engineering dutiable.
  49. The correct answer is A (Transaction value of the imported merchandise, the statute's primary basis): 19 USC 1401a sets transaction value of the imported merchandise as the first method in the hierarchy, and CBP must use it whenever a bona fide sale for exportation to the United States exists, a price actually paid or payable can be identified, and no limitation applies. This entry shows an actual commercial sale with a clear price and no disqualifying restriction. The later methods are reached only when the earlier method cannot be applied. The preferred basis governs while it remains available.
  50. The correct answer is B (The proposal is prohibited because a higher of two alternative values cannot be used): the fallback method permits a reasonable adjustment of one of the preceding methods, but 19 USC 1401a(f) expressly bars a system using the higher of two alternative values. A fallback value also cannot rest on minimum values or arbitrary figures. The importer's proposal would adopt exactly the prohibited approach. The broker must reject it and select a permissible adjusted basis.
  51. The correct answer is A ($62,000.00, the factory's price under the first sale doctrine): When the first sale in a multi-tiered transaction was a bona fide sale, the goods were clearly destined for exportation to the United States at the time of that sale, and the sale was conducted at arm's length, the price of that earlier sale may serve as the transaction value. The importer documented all three conditions for the factory sale, so the $62,000.00 price may be used instead of the $80,000.00 paid to the middleman. Using the earlier price strips out the middleman's markup and generally produces a lower dutiable value. Averaging the two prices has no basis in the statute.
  52. The correct answer is B (At the time the importer files the entry summary with CBP): 19 USC 1401a normally applies deductive value before computed value, but it lets the importer elect to reverse that order. The election must be made when the entry summary is filed, not later in the entry process. Because this importer holds the foreign producer's cost records, computed value is the method with the better supporting data. Making the election at the proper time preserves the importer's right to use it.
  53. The correct answer is D (It is excluded because separately itemized post-importation assembly and technical assistance is not dutiable): the statutory additions in 19 USC 1401a(b)(1) cover only the listed items, and charges for construction, assembly, and technical assistance performed in the United States after importation are excluded when they are separately itemized. The seller's technicians assembled and tested the line after entry. The $45,000.00 charge is separately stated on the documents. It therefore does not enter transaction value.
  54. The correct answer is A (Deduct an additional amount for the value added by the importer's further processing): When the imported merchandise is sold in the United States only after further processing, the deductive value variant allows an additional deduction for the value added by that processing. The tubing was cut, bent, and welded into bicycle frames before resale, so the value the importer added through that work comes out of the resale price along with the usual deductions. Using the unadjusted resale price would credit the imported tubing with value created after importation. Adding the processing value to the transaction price moves in the wrong direction, because deductive value works backward from the resale price.
  55. The correct answer is C (Transaction value fails because resale proceeds accrue to the seller and cannot be adjusted): the fifth statutory addition covers proceeds of a subsequent resale that accrue to the seller, but 19 USC 1401a(b)(2) removes transaction value when part of the resale proceeds accrues to the seller and cannot be adjusted for under that addition. The twenty percent share cannot be calculated when the entry is filed. The result does not depend on the size of the share or on the parties' relationship. CBP must move to the next appraisement method.
  56. The correct answer is A (Transaction value, deductive value, or computed value of identical or similar goods at about that time): The test values comparison asks whether the transaction value closely approximates the transaction value of identical or similar merchandise sold to unrelated buyers, the deductive value of identical or similar merchandise, or the computed value of identical or similar merchandise. The comparison must be made at or about the same time as the importation being appraised. The producer's home market price, a United States retail price after importation, and prices charged to affiliates are not the comparison figures the statute describes. Satisfying this test is an alternative to the circumstances of the sale test, and only one of the two is needed.
  57. The correct answer is C (It allows transaction value because the restriction does not substantially affect value): 19 USC 1401a(b)(2) disqualifies restrictions on the buyer's disposition or use unless the restriction does not substantially affect the value of the merchandise. The serial plate restriction leaves the generators usable and marketable, so value is not materially reduced. CBP approval of the contract is not a condition the statute imposes. The broker may proceed with transaction value.
  58. The correct answer is A (They are related, because one party controls the other through majority ownership): The related-party categories include one party controlling the other, and eighty percent ownership of the importer's voting stock together with direction of its purchasing decisions establishes that control. The five percent threshold category would also be met, since the producer's holding far exceeds five percent of the importer's voting stock. Separate legal identity in different countries does not remove the relationship, and a single supplier arrangement is not what creates it. The consequence is that CBP will scrutinize the price, and transaction value survives only if one of the two statutory tests is satisfied.
  59. The correct answer is C (A reasonable adjustment of one of the preceding statutory methods): the fallback method applies only after transaction value, identical merchandise, similar merchandise, deductive value, and computed value all fail. 19 USC 1401a(f) then directs CBP to derive value through a reasonable adjustment of one of those preceding methods. The adjustment remains subject to the statute's prohibited approaches. A negotiated price, a tariff minimum, or the highest prior figure does not satisfy that requirement.
  60. The correct answer is B ($1,500.00, applying the rate to the value converted at the certified rate): The invoice must first be converted to United States dollars using the certified exchange rate for the date of exportation, so 40,000 units at 1.25 dollars per unit equals $50,000.00. Applying the 3.0 percent ad valorem rate to that converted value gives $1,500.00. Applying the duty rate to the foreign currency figure directly ignores the conversion step and understates the duty. Multiplying by the exchange rate alone, without applying the duty rate, does not produce a duty amount.
  61. The correct answer is B (The Federal Reserve Bank of New York, through its published certified rate): The statute directs that the certified rate of exchange published by the Federal Reserve Bank of New York for the date of exportation be used to convert foreign currency invoices. That published rate is the reference point for the conversion, rather than a rate chosen by the parties or by a foreign authority. The Treasury Department, the foreign central bank, and the exporting country's customs authority are not the sources the valuation statute names. Documenting the rate from the correct source supports the entered value if CBP later questions the conversion.
  62. The correct answer is C (The first sale must be bona fide, at arm's length, and destined for the United States): Under the first sale doctrine, an earlier sale in the chain may supply the transaction value when that sale was a bona fide sale, the goods were clearly destined for exportation to the United States at the time of that sale, and the sale was conducted at arm's length without non-market influence on price. All three conditions apply to the first sale rather than to the last sale in the chain. There is no requirement that the middleman hold title for a set period, and no requirement that the middleman add no value. The broker must document the three conditions to support the lower value.
  63. The correct answer is B (The date of exportation of the merchandise from the foreign country): Under 19 CFR 159.31 through 159.36, a foreign currency invoice is converted using the certified rate of exchange in effect on the date of exportation of the merchandise. The invoice date, the date of entry, and the date the payment clears are not the reference points the regulations use. Using a rate for the wrong date changes the dollar value of the merchandise and therefore the duty owed. The broker should confirm the exportation date on the shipment records before selecting the rate.
  64. The correct answer is C (The rate applicable to the specific kind of transaction being converted): When more than one rate is certified for a date, CBP uses the rate applicable to the specific kind of transaction involved. The importer's ordinary commercial purchase is a particular kind of transaction, so the broker must match the rate to that transaction rather than average the available rates. Choosing the highest rate to be conservative is not authorized, and switching to the rate for the filing date departs from the date of exportation rule. Correct documentation of the chosen rate matters because the rate directly affects the converted dutiable value.
  65. The correct answer is D (Materials and fabrication, profit and general expenses, assists, and packing costs): Computed value under 19 USC 1401a(e) is the sum of the cost or value of materials and fabrication or other processing used in producing the merchandise, an amount for profit and general expenses usually reflected in sales of merchandise of the same class or kind, the value of any assists not already included, and the cost of packing. Each of those four elements belongs in the total, so a correct computed value analysis gathers all of them from the producer's records. The United States resale price less profit and general expenses describes deductive value rather than computed value. Post-importation value and the importer's domestic selling expenses play no part in a computed value build-up.
  66. The correct answer is C (Computed value relies on the producer's cost records, which the importer can obtain): Computed value is built from the producer's cost structure, so it requires access to the foreign producer's cost records, which is harder to obtain than a resale price. An importer with a related-party manufacturing relationship may have close visibility into those manufacturing costs, while lacking dependable United States resale data for a deductive value analysis. The statute allows the importer to elect computed value before deductive value at the time of filing the entry summary for that reason. Computed value is not the first method in the hierarchy, and it does not depend on the parties being unrelated.
  67. The correct answer is B (Transaction value cannot be used, and CBP moves to the next method): Transaction value cannot be used when the buyer and seller are related and the relationship influenced the price, unless the importer shows that one of the two statutory tests is satisfied. A price at half the level charged to unrelated buyers indicates that the relationship affected the price, and the importer has no offsetting showing. A written contract does not preserve transaction value, and related parties cannot set the price without consequence when the relationship influences it. Because no basis is shown for keeping transaction value, CBP moves to the next method in the hierarchy, which is transaction value of identical merchandise.
  68. The correct answer is C (Sales to the unrelated independent retailers, because the statute looks to unrelated buyers): Deductive value starts from the unit price at which the merchandise in its imported condition is sold in the United States to unrelated buyers. Sales to a wholesale affiliate the importer controls are not sales to unrelated buyers, so volume alone does not make them the proper measure. The independent retailers are unrelated buyers, and their transactions can support the analysis even though they represent a smaller quantity. The existence of a separate corporate form for the affiliate does not make that buyer unrelated.
  69. The correct answer is B (The price was set consistent with industry practice, the seller's unrelated sales, and full cost recovery): Under the circumstances of the sale test, the importer shows that the price was set in a manner consistent with normal industry pricing practices, consistent with how the seller prices sales to unrelated buyers, and adequate to recover all costs plus a profit consistent with the firm's overall profit on merchandise of the same class or kind. Those three elements together describe how the price was arrived at, which is the focus of the test. Government certification and an exact match to deductive value are not part of the test. The test values comparison is the separate alternative path, which compares the price against transaction value, deductive value, or computed value of identical or similar merchandise.
  70. The correct answer is D (Transaction value does not apply, because the condition has no determinable value): Transaction value cannot be used when the sale or price is subject to a condition or consideration for which a value cannot be determined. The importer's promise of unspecified future technical support is exactly that kind of condition, because no amount can be assigned to it. The existence of a stated price does not save transaction value when the price is bound to an unquantifiable condition. Whether the promise was written and when the support would be delivered do not change the analysis, since the barrier is the absence of a determinable value.
  71. The correct answer is B (The clause does not bar transaction value, because it does not affect value): Transaction value survives a restriction on the buyer's disposition or use when the restriction does not substantially affect the value of the merchandise. The clause confines the olive oil to the importer's own stores, and the facts state that it does not reduce what buyers will pay. The reason is the absence of any effect on value rather than who owns the retail outlets. A restriction that materially depressed the worth of the goods would fall outside the exception and could disqualify transaction value.
  72. The correct answer is D (Transaction value remains available, because a resale area restriction is excepted): Restrictions that merely limit the geographic area in which the buyer may resell the merchandise are among the exceptions that do not bar transaction value. The agreement confines resales to the western states, which is exactly the kind of territorial limit the statute tolerates. Nothing in the facts suggests the restriction substantially affects value, and the price was set and paid in the ordinary way. The existence of a written distribution agreement does not by itself disqualify the price that was actually paid.
  73. The correct answer is C (Usual commission or profit and general expenses, post-importation transport, and import duties): Deductive value at 19 USC 1401a(d) starts with the United States resale price for the greatest aggregate quantity of the merchandise sold in its imported condition to unrelated buyers. From that price the statute allows deduction of the usual commission or the usual profit and general expenses for the same class or kind of merchandise, the usual costs of transportation and insurance incurred after importation, and customs duties and federal taxes payable by reason of importation. The producer's material and fabrication costs belong to computed value, and the price actually paid or payable belongs to transaction value. Assists are built into dutiable value rather than deducted from a resale price.
  74. The correct answer is D (The shared profit is added as proceeds of resale accruing to the seller): The fifth statutory addition at 19 USC 1401a(b)(1) covers the proceeds of any subsequent resale, disposal, or use of the imported merchandise that accrue, directly or indirectly, to the seller. The twenty percent profit share accrues to the seller from the United States resale and is added once the amount can be determined, which it is here because the resale price is known. The addition applies rather than disqualifying transaction value, because the amount can be adjusted for under that addition. A buying commission is a fee paid to the buyer's own agent, which is a different item entirely.
  75. The correct answer is C (Transaction value survives, because satisfying either statutory test is sufficient): Where the buyer and seller are related, transaction value may still be used if the importer shows either the circumstances of the sale test or the test values comparison. The importer here can show that the price closely approximates the transaction value of similar merchandise sold to unrelated buyers at about the same time, which satisfies the test values comparison. Only one of the two tests needs to be satisfied, so the missing evidence on industry pricing does not defeat the importer. Related parties are not exempt from the tests, which is why the showing was necessary in the first place.
  76. The correct answer is D (It is excluded, because the fee is for the right to resell or distribute): A royalty paid only for the right to resell or distribute goods, unconnected to the sale of the goods themselves, is generally not added to transaction value. This fee buys distribution rights for the roaster's product line, would continue regardless of any single shipment, and is not a condition of the sale of the merchandise being appraised. The frequency of payment is not the reason for the exclusion, since the test looks at what the payment is for. The two conditions for dutiability, a relation to the merchandise and a link to the sale, are both absent here.
  77. The correct answer is A (The price actually paid or payable for merchandise sold for exportation to the United States): Transaction value begins with the price actually paid or payable for merchandise sold for exportation to the United States, and the five statutory additions are then added to the extent they are not already included and are quantifiable. The producer's costs belong to computed value, and the price in the producer's home market is a figure the fallback method forbids. The price at which the goods sell in the United States in the greatest aggregate quantity is the starting point of deductive value. Each of those alternatives is a separate method, not the definition of transaction value.
  78. The correct answer is D (It is added as an assist because merchandise consumed in production qualifies): The definition of an assist includes merchandise consumed in producing the imported merchandise for export, in addition to materials, components, and parts incorporated into the finished goods. The catalyst was supplied by the buyer free of charge and was used up in production, so its value is an assist even though nothing of it remains in the cylinders. Being consumed rather than incorporated does not move the item into the seller's ordinary overhead, because the buyer bore the cost. A selling commission, by contrast, is a fee paid to an agent representing the seller.
  79. The correct answer is B (The production dies supplied free of charge for use in producing the goods): An assist is anything the buyer supplies free of charge or at reduced cost for use in producing or selling the imported merchandise, and tools, dies, and molds used in production are expressly listed at 19 USC 1401a(h)(1). The dies in this importation fit that definition because the buyer supplied them without charge for use in making the goods. International freight is a cost of transportation that is excluded when itemized separately, and a fee paid to the buyer's own purchasing agent is a buying commission that is also excluded. Import duties paid to CBP are not part of dutiable value either.
  80. The correct answer is C (It does not bar transaction value, because restrictions imposed by law are excepted): Transaction value remains available despite restrictions on the buyer's disposition or use when the restriction is imposed by law. The exception also covers restrictions that limit the geographic area of resale and restrictions that do not substantially affect the value of the merchandise. A legal restriction on resale of these pharmaceuticals therefore falls within the exception and does not disqualify the sale. The presence of a sale and a price is what allows the inquiry to reach the restriction rules in the first place.
  81. The correct answer is A (The price of goods sold for export to a country other than the United States): The fallback method cannot be based on the price of merchandise sold for export to a country other than the United States, because that price reflects a different export market and different terms of sale. The domestic selling price of merchandise produced in the United States, the producer's home market price, and the price of sales made to another country are all listed prohibitions. Adding freight to the price of goods sold to a third country does not convert a forbidden figure into an acceptable one. A broker facing this situation should look to a reasonable adjustment of one of the five statutory bases instead.
  82. The correct answer is C ($15,000.00, the invoice price plus the apportioned share of the mold): The value of an assist is apportioned reasonably, commonly across the first shipment or the entire anticipated production run, and applied consistently. Dividing the $24,000.00 mold value across 6,000 anticipated units gives $4.00 per unit, so the 1,500 units in the first shipment carry $6,000.00 of assist value. Adding that share to the $9,000.00 invoice price gives a transaction value of $15,000.00. Adding the mold's full value, or adding an arbitrary fraction of the invoice price, does not follow the apportionment rule.
  83. The correct answer is D (It is added to the price actually paid or payable as a buyer-paid packing cost): Packing costs incurred by the buyer, including containers, coverings, and the labor and materials used to pack the merchandise for export, are one of the five additions listed at 19 USC 1401a(b)(1). The addition applies precisely because the buyer, rather than the seller, bore the cost, so the amount never entered the invoiced price. Packing for export is not a post-importation service, and it is not a commission paid to an agent. The crates and packing labor must therefore be added to the price actually paid or payable.

Practice Test 7 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is C (Who controls the agent, who bears the risk, and how the fee tracks seller profit): CBP looks at who controls the agent, whether the agent bears risk, and whether the fee tracks the profitability of the sale to the manufacturer when telling a buying commission from a selling commission. Those factors show whose interests the agent actually serves, which is the substance of the inquiry. The label chosen by the parties, the form of the fee as a percentage or a flat amount, and the currency of payment do not decide the question. The timing of payment relative to customs clearance is likewise not a listed factor.
  2. The correct answer is B ($42,000.00, because payments to be made by the buyer are counted): The definition of the price actually paid or payable covers the total payment made, or to be made, by the buyer to or for the benefit of the seller. A deferred installment for the same merchandise is therefore part of the price even though it is paid after the goods arrive. The timing of the payment does not remove it from the price, and the later installment is not a royalty because it is owed for the goods themselves rather than for a right connected to them. Reading the two installment amounts together gives a price of $42,000.00.
  3. The correct answer is D (Minimum values, and arbitrary or fictitious values, may not be used): The fallback method expressly forbids appraisement on the basis of minimum values or of arbitrary or fictitious values. A published floor tied to average import prices is a minimum value by another name, so it cannot substitute for a real appraisement basis. The absence of other data does not permit an invented figure, and the importer's agreement cannot make a forbidden approach lawful. A proper fallback appraisement adjusts one of the preceding statutory methods with reasonable flexibility.
  4. The correct answer is A (Because Congress intended United States valuation law to reflect the actual commercial reality of each transaction rather than administratively fixed floors disconnected from real transaction data): The Trade Agreements Act of 1979 implemented the WTO Customs Valuation Agreement's core principle that dutiable value should be based on the real commercial price of the actual transaction, or a reasonable proxy tied to real market data, rather than administratively fixed minimum or arbitrary values that bear no necessary relationship to the true value of the specific goods. This prohibition applies broadly and is not limited by the trading partner's normal trade relations status. It is not that minimum values were empirically found to always overstate value; the prohibition exists as a matter of legal principle regardless of whether a minimum value happens to be higher or lower than the true value, and importer consent cannot revive an otherwise prohibited method.
  5. The correct answer is D (A reasonable adjustment of one of the preceding appraisement methods): When none of the first five methods can be used, the statute falls back to a reasonable adjustment of one of the preceding methods rather than to an open estimate. The domestic price in the exporting country, the higher of two alternative values, and published minimum values are all approaches the statute forbids in a fallback appraisement. The fallback method still has to stay anchored to one of the five statutory bases so that the result can be defended. A broker should document which preceding method was adjusted and how the adjustment was made.
  6. The correct answer is A (Whether the seller could refuse to sell the imported merchandise to the buyer if the buyer did not agree to make the payment): The central inquiry in determining whether a payment functions as a dutiable royalty or license fee under the condition-of-sale test is whether the buyer's ability to purchase the imported merchandise is contingent on making that payment, meaning the seller could decline to sell the goods absent payment. The literal label used in the contract, such as whether the word royalty appears, is not controlling because parties can mislabel or fail to label a genuinely conditional payment. Similarly, the currency of payment and the frequency of remittance, whether monthly or annual, do not determine dutiability; only the substantive relationship between the payment and the right to purchase the goods matters.
  7. The correct answer is C (The price in the exporting country's domestic market may not be used): The fallback method may not be based on the price of the merchandise in the exporting country's domestic market, because that market does not reflect the sale for exportation being appraised. Removing the producer's profit from the home market price leaves a derived cost figure that the fallback method does not authorize either. A lower home market price is no more usable than a higher one, since the prohibition applies to the source of the figure rather than to its size. The correct path is a reasonable adjustment of one of the five statutory bases.
  8. The correct answer is B (Computed value): 19 USC 1401a(a)(2) permits the importer to request that computed value be applied before deductive value, reversing what would otherwise be the default order, provided the importer can furnish CBP with the foreign producer's cost data needed to compute that value. This election is unique to the deductive value and computed value pairing; there is no similar importer election available to reorder transaction value of identical or similar merchandise ahead of one another, or to elevate the fallback method ahead of the other secondary methods. If the importer cannot supply adequate computed value information after requesting this order, CBP may revert to deductive value.
  9. The correct answer is D (Merchandise the same in all respects, including physical characteristics, quality, and reputation): Identical merchandise means merchandise the same in all respects as the goods being appraised, including physical characteristics, quality, and reputation, and it must be produced in the same country, preferably by the same producer. Merchandise that is merely like or commercially interchangeable is similar merchandise, a separate and lower basis in the hierarchy. Resales in the United States in the imported condition describe the starting point of deductive value rather than identical merchandise. The radiators in this comparison fit the identical merchandise standard because they match on every listed attribute and came from the same producer.
  10. The correct answer is A (Sales at the same commercial level and in substantially the same quantity as the sale of the merchandise being appraised are preferred, with adjustments permitted for differences): For transaction value of identical or similar merchandise, CBP looks for sales at the same commercial level of trade and in substantially the same quantity as the transaction being appraised, and if such a sale is not available, adjustments may be made to account for differences in commercial level or quantity, provided the adjustments are based on sufficient evidence. There is no requirement limiting the comparison to the exact same calendar day, and the comparison sale need not involve the identical United States buyer. Retail-level and wholesale-level sales are not interchangeable without appropriate adjustment because differing trade levels typically reflect materially different pricing.
  11. The correct answer is B (A bona fide discount actually reflected in the price paid or payable generally reduces the dutiable value, provided it is not designed to avoid duties and is supported by adequate documentation): A genuine, bona fide discount that is actually reflected in the price the buyer pays generally reduces the transaction value, since transaction value is based on the price actually paid or payable, which already accounts for legitimate price adjustments such as volume or early payment discounts. CBP will scrutinize discounts that appear designed artificially to reduce dutiable value without an underlying commercial rationale, but a properly documented, bona fide discount is not added back. There is no rule confining discount recognition to temporary importation bond entries, and a discount does not by itself force a switch away from transaction value to the fallback method.
  12. The correct answer is D (Transaction value of the imported merchandise, identical merchandise, similar merchandise, deductive value, computed value): 19 USC 1401a sets out five bases of appraisement applied in a fixed order, beginning with transaction value of the imported merchandise itself as the preferred method. If that method cannot be used, CBP moves to transaction value of identical merchandise and then to transaction value of similar merchandise. Only when none of the first three methods can be applied does CBP turn to deductive value and computed value, ordinarily in that order. The fallback method is reached only when none of those five bases applies.
  13. The correct answer is B (Transaction value of the imported merchandise): Transaction value requires an actual sale for exportation to the United States, so consigned goods, which are shipped without a completed sale, cannot be appraised using transaction value of the imported merchandise. CBP must instead move to the alternative methods of appraisement in their statutory order, beginning with transaction value of identical or similar merchandise, and continuing through deductive value, computed value, or the fallback method as appropriate. Deductive value, computed value, and the fallback method do not require that the specific imported merchandise itself have been the subject of a completed sale, which is why they remain available even for consigned shipments.
  14. The correct answer is A (It examines whether the price was settled in a manner consistent with the normal pricing practices of the industry or in a way that adequately recovers all costs plus a profit, to determine whether the relationship influenced the price): The circumstances-of-sale test looks at factors such as whether the price was settled in a manner consistent with the normal pricing practices of the industry in question, or whether the price is adequate to ensure recovery of all costs plus a profit equivalent to the firm's overall profit realized over a representative period of time on sales of merchandise of the same class or kind. This test exists specifically to determine, in a related-party context, whether the relationship between buyer and seller influenced the price actually paid or payable. It has no connection to free trade agreement eligibility, currency conversion rates, or assist apportionment methodology, which are governed by separate rules.
  15. The correct answer is B (A reasonable adjustment of one of the other statutory methods of appraisement, applied in a manner consistent with the general principles of the valuation statute): The fallback method allows appraisement using a reasonable adjustment of one of the other prescribed valuation methods, such as transaction value, deductive value, or computed value, applied flexibly enough to reach a value when none of the other methods can be strictly applied. It is not unlimited discretion, nor is it based on domestic wholesale prices of competing United States-made goods or on any fixed minimum value schedule, both of which are expressly prohibited bases of appraisement under the statute. The fallback method still must be reasonable and consistent with the general principles and requirements of the valuation statute and the Trade Agreements Act.
  16. The correct answer is A (19 USC 1401a, enacted as part of the Trade Agreements Act of 1979): 19 USC 1401a, enacted as part of the Trade Agreements Act of 1979, establishes the methods of appraisement used to determine the dutiable value of imported merchandise, implementing the WTO Customs Valuation Agreement in United States law. Section 1592 addresses civil penalties for false statements or omissions in connection with importation, section 1484 addresses the entry process itself, and section 1514 governs the protest procedure for challenging CBP decisions. Valuation questions on the exam should be traced specifically to 19 USC 1401a rather than these related but distinct provisions.
  17. The correct answer is B (The goods were clearly destined for export to the United States at the time of the earlier sale, and that sale was a bona fide, arm's length transaction): To use the first sale rule, the importer must establish that the goods were clearly destined for export to the United States at the time of the earlier sale in the chain and that the earlier sale was conducted at arm's length, free from any distortion caused by a relationship between the parties. There is no requirement that the earlier seller and the ultimate United States importer be entirely unrelated, though a related-party relationship at any tier triggers the circumstances-of-sale analysis. There is no requirement that the invoice be denominated in United States dollars, nor a fixed calendar-year timing requirement. CBP places the burden of proof on the importer to produce documentation supporting each element.
  18. The correct answer is C (There are restrictions on the disposition or use of the merchandise by the buyer, other than restrictions imposed by law or that limit the geographic area in which the goods may be resold, or that do not substantially affect the value of the merchandise): Transaction value cannot be used, apart from the related-party rules, when there are restrictions on the disposition or use of the merchandise by the buyer that are not imposed or required by law, do not limit the geographic area in which the goods may be resold, and do substantially affect the value of the merchandise, or when the sale is subject to a condition or consideration for which a value cannot be determined. Simply negotiating a better price than a competitor paid, using a letter of credit rather than open account terms, or choosing air freight over ocean freight are ordinary commercial variables that do not disqualify transaction value. The disqualifying restrictions must be significant and not among the narrow categories of restrictions the statute permits.
  19. The correct answer is B (Materials, tools, dies, molds, and similar items, or engineering and development work performed outside the United States and necessary for production): An assist under 19 USC 1401a(h)(1) includes materials, components, parts and similar items incorporated in the imported merchandise, tools, dies, molds and similar items used in producing the merchandise, materials consumed in production, and engineering, development, artwork, design work and plans and sketches undertaken outside the United States and necessary for production. Domestic marketing, general overhead, and post-importation warehousing are not assists because they are not directly tied to the production of the specific imported merchandise. Only items supplied free of charge or at reduced cost by the buyer, directly or indirectly, for use in production qualify.
  20. The correct answer is C (The value of a quota charge is generally included in dutiable value if it is part of the price paid to the seller for the merchandise): When a payment for a quota right or charge is bundled into the price the buyer pays the seller for the merchandise, that amount is generally considered part of the price actually paid or payable and is included in dutiable transaction value, since it is a cost the buyer incurs to obtain the goods themselves. This differs from international freight and insurance, which are excluded from dutiable value because they relate to transportation rather than the acquisition of the merchandise. The dutiability of a quota charge does not depend on whether the ultimate recipient of the funds is a government agency or a private party; what matters is whether the payment is part of what the buyer pays for the goods.
  21. The correct answer is B (The value of any assist supplied by the buyer free of charge or at reduced cost for use in producing the merchandise): The five statutory additions are packing costs incurred by the buyer, any selling commission paid by the buyer, the value of any assist, royalties or license fees the buyer must pay as a condition of sale, and the value of any proceeds of a subsequent resale accruing to the seller. International freight and insurance are generally excluded from dutiable value when the merchandise is appraised on a first-sale or ex-works basis rather than a CIF or landed basis. Customs brokerage fees for entry processing in the United States are likewise not part of the statutory additions.
  22. The correct answer is D (Demonstrating that the transaction value closely approximates a test value, such as the transaction value in sales of identical or similar merchandise to unrelated buyers at or about the same time): Even where the circumstances of the sale do not affirmatively show the relationship left the price unaffected, transaction value may still be used if it closely approximates one of several test values specified in the statute, most commonly the transaction value of identical or similar merchandise in sales to unrelated buyers in the United States at or about the same time. A foreign customs authority waiver, an arbitrary percentage reduction, or a change to origin marking have no bearing on whether the related-party price qualifies for use as transaction value under United States law. The test-value comparison gives related parties an objective, evidence-based alternative path to the circumstances-of-sale analysis.
  23. The correct answer is C (International freight and insurance charges for transporting the merchandise from the country of exportation to the United States): International freight and insurance charges incurred in transporting merchandise from the country of exportation to the place of importation in the United States are excluded from transaction value, provided they are identified separately from the price actually paid or payable, since dutiable value is generally assessed on an ex-factory or FOB basis rather than a CIF basis. Assists, royalties required as a condition of sale, and selling commissions, by contrast, remain part of the statutory additions that must be added to transaction value regardless of how they are itemized on the invoice. This distinction reflects that freight and insurance relate to the cost of transportation rather than to the value of the merchandise itself.
  24. The correct answer is A (It is excluded from the value of an assist under the statutory carve-out for domestic engineering and design work): 19 USC 1401a(h)(1)(A) specifically excludes engineering, development, artwork, design work, and plans and sketches undertaken within the United States from the definition of an assist. This carve-out exists because Congress wanted to avoid discouraging domestic engineering and design activity by making it dutiable. Only such work undertaken outside the United States and necessary for the production of the imported merchandise counts as an assist requiring an addition to transaction value. This distinction is frequently tested because it is counterintuitive relative to the treatment of physical assists like tooling.
  25. The correct answer is C (Merchandise produced in the same country as the merchandise being appraised that is like the appraised merchandise in characteristics and component materials, and is commercially interchangeable with it, though not identical in all respects): Similar merchandise is merchandise produced in the same country as, and by the same producer if possible as, the merchandise being appraised, that is like the appraised merchandise in characteristics and component materials and is commercially interchangeable with it, even though the goods are not identical in every respect. Sharing a brand name alone, entering through the same port, or carrying an identical retail price does not establish commercial interchangeability or the required similarity in characteristics and materials. Commercial interchangeability is the operative legal standard, assessed based on quality, reputation, and the existence of a trademark, among other factors.
  26. The correct answer is A (The total payment made or to be made by the buyer to or for the benefit of the seller for the imported merchandise): The price actually paid or payable is defined as the total payment made, or to be made, by the buyer to or for the benefit of the seller for the imported merchandise, whether made directly or indirectly. This total includes all payments made as a condition of sale, even those paid to a party other than the seller if made for the seller's benefit. It is not limited to what appears on the commercial invoice alone. From this base figure, the five statutory additions under 19 USC 1401a(b)(1) are then added if applicable.
  27. The correct answer is C (The cost of all containers and coverings, and of packing, whether for labor or materials, incurred by the buyer with respect to the imported merchandise): Packing costs as a statutory addition refer to the cost of containers, coverings, and the labor and materials used to pack the merchandise for its journey, when those costs are incurred by the buyer and not already reflected in the price actually paid or payable. Post-arrival domestic warehousing, in-transit insurance, and port-of-entry unpacking or inspection costs are distinct expenses governed by other rules or excluded from dutiable value altogether. Packing costs are added regardless of whether they are separately itemized on the invoice or absorbed into the unit price, so long as they are not already included.
  28. The correct answer is D (The buyer and seller are officers or directors of one another's businesses): 19 USC 1401a(g) defines related parties to include, among other relationships, members of the same family, an officer or director of one organization who is also an officer or director of the other, business partners, and an employer and employee relationship, as well as a person who owns, controls, or holds the power to vote five percent or more of an organization's outstanding voting stock, which makes that person related to that organization. Simply having a long-standing business relationship, sharing a country of export, or using a common freight forwarder does not, by itself, create a related-party relationship. The related-party definition is specific and exhaustive under the statute rather than a general standard of familiarity.
  29. The correct answer is C (The sale must be a sale of the particular merchandise for exportation to the United States): Transaction value requires that there be an actual sale of the specific merchandise being appraised for exportation to the United States; consigned goods, goods imported for further manufacture without a completed sale, and free samples generally do not qualify because no bona fide sale for exportation has occurred. There is no statutory requirement tied to a particular fiscal quarter, a letter of credit from a United States bank, or full advance payment; these are merely common commercial practices that do not by themselves establish or negate transaction value. The key legal requirement is the existence of a genuine sale transaction directed toward exportation to the United States.
  30. Applying the 5.5% ad valorem rate to $66,240.00 gives a duty owed of $3,643.20.
  31. The correct answer is C (Commissions usually paid, or an amount for profit and general expenses usually reflected in the class or kind of merchandise sold, plus freight, insurance, and other expenses incurred after importation): Proper deductions from the United States resale price in computing deductive value include the usual commission or profit and general expenses associated with sales of that class or kind of merchandise, freight, insurance, and other expenses incurred after importation, and United States customs duties and federal taxes applicable to the merchandise. Assist values and royalties relate to transaction value or computed value calculations rather than deductive value's resale-based deduction scheme. Foreign manufacturer advertising expenses incurred before export are not part of the deductive value deduction list either, since deductive value works forward from the domestic resale price.

  34. The correct answer is B ($15,096.00): Deductive value starts from the unit price at which the goods are sold in the United States in the greatest aggregate quantity and deducts the usual commission or profit and general expenses, as well as costs incurred after importation such as freight and insurance, per 19 USC 1401a(d). Here $2,304.00 in commission and $1,800.00 in post-importation freight and insurance are subtracted from the $19,200.00 resale total. $19,200.00 minus $2,304.00 minus $1,800.00 equals $15,096.00.
  35. The correct answer is C (When the value of the proceeds accrues directly or indirectly to the seller): Under 19 USC 1401a(b)(1)(E), any part of the proceeds of a subsequent resale, disposal, or use of the imported merchandise that accrues, directly or indirectly, to the seller is added to the price actually paid or payable. This is distinct from ordinary resale profit retained entirely by the importer, which is not dutiable at all because it never flows back to the foreign seller. There is no statutory time limit tied to thirty days, and no threshold requiring the resale price to double the transaction value. The key test is whether the seller receives a share of the resale proceeds under the terms of the sale.
